CAVES OF OOO
DIALOGUE IN STORY ORDER

A complete reading script of the authored conversations, with branching choices, optional stories and all four endings.
Read chapters 1–12 as progression toward the final choice. Chapters 13–16 are mutually exclusive endings. Chapters 17–18 hold ambient services and additional authored scenarios. Optional chapters can occur in another order during play.
D001-style labels identify dialogue nodes; player choices point to the next node. R001-style labels identify runtime variants and interactions. Availability notes are not spoken dialogue. Text in braces is supplied by the world or current quest state.
Source: main · e5a782a2e8d9 · compiled 10 November 2026.

CONTENTS

========================================================================
01 · ARRIVAL IN THE SPREAD
========================================================================
  [Opening]
The current fresh game begins in a Spread clearing, normally east of Sill. The clearing warden gives a geographic lead; field residents explain ordinary survival and useful places. This precedes the Sill omen.


The clearing warden: current opening

  [Describe / Start]
  [Reference glade; living nonhostile participants. {geographic_lead} uses nearest current geographic destination or no-directions fallback.]
I keep watch over this clearing. Passing through, or looking for somewhere to go?

Warden — base and village-location wording
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Wardens.json · Warden_1]

D001 · Opening / return
WARDEN:
Halt, traveler. I am the warden of this village. State your business.

  PLAYER 1: I'm just passing through.
    Continue at D002 — Passing Through.
  PLAYER 2: Tell me about this village.
    Continue at D003 — About Village.
  PLAYER 3: Are there any threats nearby?
    Continue at D004 — Threats.
  PLAYER 4: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D002 · Passing Through
WARDEN:
Then pass through peacefully. We don't tolerate troublemakers here, but honest travelers are welcome. The elder can tell you more about the area if you need guidance.

  PLAYER 1: Thanks for the welcome.
    Continue at D001 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D003 · About Village
WARDEN:
This is a small settlement, but we look after our own. The elder leads us, the merchant trades goods, and I keep watch. We've had our share of trouble from the wilds, but we endure.

  PLAYER 1: What kind of trouble?
    Continue at D004 — Threats.
  PLAYER 2: Sounds like a good village.
    Continue at D001 — Start.
  PLAYER 3: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D004 · Threats
WARDEN:
The usual -- beasts from the wilds, bandits on the roads. Lately the creatures have been bolder. Something's stirring them up. I've doubled my patrols, but one warden can only do so much.

  PLAYER 1: I could help deal with some of those threats.
    Continue at D005 — Offer Help.
  PLAYER 2: What kind of creatures?
    Continue at D006 — Creatures.
  PLAYER 3: Stay vigilant, warden.
    Conversation ends.

D005 · Offer Help
WARDEN:
A willing blade is always welcome. Clear out some of the beasts near the village and you'll have our gratitude -- and maybe a reward from the elder.

  PLAYER 1: Consider it done.
    Conversation ends.
After this choice: The warden nods with respect.

  PLAYER 2: I'll see what I can do.
    Continue at D001 — Start.

D006 · Creatures
WARDEN:
Depends on where you are. Near the caves it's marlbacks and bats. The desert has scorpions and bandits. The jungle is full of spiders and vipers. And the old ruins... I don't like to think about what lurks in the ruins.

  PLAYER 1: I can handle myself.
    Continue at D001 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Thanks for the warning.
    Conversation ends.

Nella Tern, seedkeeper
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/SpreadFieldResidents.json · SpreadSeedKeeper_1 · Connected seedkeeper plot]

D007 · Opening / return
NELLA TERN, SEEDKEEPER:
Mind the little rows. Gladroot in one, emberwheat in the other. You can tend what's here, or buy seed and start a patch of your own.

  PLAYER 1: How do I plant a patch?
    Continue at D008 — Planting.
  PLAYER 2: Have you heard of any useful places or routes?
    Continue at D011 — Nearby.
  PLAYER 3: What do these rows need?
    Continue at D009 — Watering.
  PLAYER 4: I'll leave you to it.
    Conversation ends.

D008 · Planting
NELLA TERN, SEEDKEEPER:
Stand on soft grass with a seed in your pack and Plant it. Leave each plant its own patch. Stone won't take it. I carry a watering grimoire too, if you've lost yours.

  PLAYER 1: How do I water it?
    Continue at D009 — Watering.
  PLAYER 2: Let me look at your pouches.
    Continue at D007 — Start.

D009 · Watering
NELLA TERN, SEEDKEEPER:
Read a watering grimoire to learn Conjure Rain, then cast near the rows. Dark soil means it's wet. Dry plants wait; they don't die. Gladroot finishes sooner. Give emberwheat another rain after the first has soaked away.

  PLAYER 1: Can I leave them growing?
    Continue at D010 — Waiting.
  PLAYER 2: All right.
    Continue at D007 — Start.

D010 · Waiting
NELLA TERN, SEEDKEEPER:
Watered plants keep growing while you travel or rest. Dry plants wait for more rain. Gladroot and emberwheat drop their food when ready; other ripe crops wait for your hand. Pick up what they leave, including saved seed. The grain can be toasted beside a cooking fire or a working oven.

  PLAYER 1: Thanks. I'll mind my feet.
    Continue at D007 — Start.

D011 · Nearby
NELLA TERN, SEEDKEEPER:
I've heard old news of places in these fields, and of a route farther afield. People move, and things get carried off.

  PLAYER 1: Back to your work.
    Continue at D007 — Start.

Orven Kett, wayside cook
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/SpreadFieldResidents.json · SpreadWaysideCook_1 · Connected wayside kitchen]

D012 · Opening / return
ORVEN KETT, WAYSIDE COOK:
Food for the road? I've a few things ready, and a few still raw. You're welcome to use the oven. Don't put your blanket over the mouth.

  PLAYER 1: What can I cook here?
    Continue at D013 — Kitchen.
  PLAYER 2: Have you heard of any useful places or routes?
    Continue at D015 — Nearby.
  PLAYER 3: May I use the cot?
    Continue at D014 — Resting.
  PLAYER 4: Good travelling.
    Conversation ends.

D013 · Kitchen
ORVEN KETT, WAYSIDE COOK:
Stand beside the oven and Cook raw meat, mushrooms or emberwheat from your pack. A whole handful goes in together. Gladroot is fine as it is. Buy something ready if you're in a hurry.

  PLAYER 1: And the cot?
    Continue at D014 — Resting.
  PLAYER 2: Let me see what you've got.
    Continue at D012 — Start.

D014 · Resting
ORVEN KETT, WAYSIDE COOK:
The cot isn't let to anyone. Rest there if it's empty and the field is quiet. You can sleep until the next part of the day, too. If there are teeth about, deal with those first.

  PLAYER 1: I'll take a look.
    Continue at D012 — Start.

D015 · Nearby
ORVEN KETT, WAYSIDE COOK:
I've heard old news of places in these fields, and of a route farther afield. People move, and things get carried off.

  PLAYER 1: Back to your work.
    Continue at D012 — Start.

R362 · Nella Tern, seedkeeper
  [HandleEvent / terms]
  [Gathering rights suspended after witnessed breach.]
Nella remembers what she saw. Two emberwheat will make amends for this breach; an old introduction will not. The open beds remain public.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/LocalGatheringClaimPart.cs:141]

R363 · Nella Tern, seedkeeper
  [HandleEvent / terms]
  [Terms while gathering rights are not suspended.]
Nella: The two tied beds and this tray are our seed reserve. Eight drams, or Orven's introduction, gives you gathering rights. The four open beds are already for anyone.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/LocalGatheringClaimPart.cs:142]

R364 · Narration
  [HandleEvent / reconcile]
  [Player makes amends with two emberwheat.]
Nella accepts the grain. You may gather from the tied row again.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/LocalGatheringClaimPart.cs:161]

R365 · Nella Tern, seedkeeper
  [HandleEvent / pay or introduction]
  [Gathering rights newly granted by payment or introduction.]
Nella agrees: the tied row and its tray are open to you. Leave the beds fit to plant again.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/LocalGatheringClaimPart.cs:161]

R366 · Narration
  [RecordBreach]
  [Nella witnesses unauthorized harvesting or reserve-tray taking.]
Nella saw you take from the tied reserve without leave. Gathering rights here are suspended; the open beds remain public.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/LocalGatheringClaimPart.cs:226]

R367 · Narration
  [Reject]
  [Requested gathering-rights arrangement unavailable.]
That arrangement is not available here. Ask Nella about the tied row.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/LocalGatheringClaimPart.cs:232]

========================================================================
02 · SILL: THE SOUND FROM A STORY
========================================================================
  [Main-story context]
Arrival in Sill triggers the one-shot sari omen. The elder, scribe and residents frame the world before the faction accounts. Knowledge-gated return topics remain later branches within these conversations.


The elder hears it
  [One shot; player enters Overworld.10.10.0.]
NARRATION:
The elder stops mid-sentence -- something in the water said a word from a story their great-grandparent told, and doesn't say it again.

  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Data/Storylets/SillHearsIt.json]

Elder
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Villagers.json · Elder_1]

D016 · Opening / return
ELDER:
Live and drink, wanderer. I am the elder of this village. What brings you to these parts?

  PLAYER 1: Tell me about this place.
    Continue at D017 — About Place.
  PLAYER 2: I could use some supplies.
    Continue at D030 — Supplies.
  PLAYER 3: I've been dealing with marlbacks nearby.
    Available when: carrying the Killed Marlback tag.
    Continue at D032 — Marlback Scrabbler Talk.
  PLAYER 4: What do you know about the factions in the deep caves?
    Available when: when Heard Faction Summary is recorded.
    Continue at D035 — Factions.
  PLAYER 5: I've learned about something called the Thinning.
    Available when: when Knows Three Endings is recorded.
    Continue at D037 — Elder Substrate.
  PLAYER 6: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D017 · About Place
ELDER:
This land was once peaceful, but the caves below have stirred. Creatures grow bolder by the day. We could use someone with your courage.

  PLAYER 1: I'll see what I can do.
    Continue at D016 — Start.
After this choice: The elder nods approvingly.

  PLAYER 2: Tell me more about the caves.
    Continue at D029 — About Caves.
  PLAYER 3: What do you mean, 'stirred'?
    Continue at D018 — Stirred.

D018 · Stirred
ELDER:
I am old, wanderer. Old enough to remember when the caves were just caves -- cold stone, blind creatures, dripping water. Something changed, years ago. A tremor in the earth, but not like any earthquake. The ground didn't shake. It... shifted. Like the world was turning in its sleep. After that, the caves became different.

  PLAYER 1: Different how?
    Continue at D019 — Different How.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D019 · Different How
ELDER:
The mushrooms came first. Growing in places nothing should grow -- through stone, through metal, through the walls of the mine. Then the lights. Faint blue glimmers deep in the tunnels, like reflections of something that isn't there. And then the creatures. Not the marlbacks -- those came later. I mean the things that think. The things that speak.

  PLAYER 1: You've encountered the Rot Choir.
    Available when: when Met Choir is recorded.
    Continue at D020 — Elder Choir.
  PLAYER 2: The blue lights might be temporal distortions.
    Available when: when Met Palimpsest is recorded.
    Continue at D026 — Elder Temporal.
  PLAYER 3: Tell me more.
    Continue at D029 — About Caves.
  PLAYER 4: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D020 · Elder Choir
ELDER:
The Rot Choir... so that is their name. I have heard them, wanderer. Late at night, when the wind is still, you can put your ear to the ground and hear... singing. Not voices, exactly. Something deeper. Like the earth itself is humming a tune. It started the same year as the tremor. The village has been uneasy ever since.

  PLAYER 1: The Choir is a mycelial consciousness. They grew from the cataclysm.
    Continue at D021 — Elder Understands.
  PLAYER 2: Are they a threat to the village?
    Continue at D025 — Elder Threat.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D021 · Elder Understands
ELDER:
A consciousness... in the fungus. I have lived a long life and thought I had seen the strangest the world had to offer. I was wrong. If what you say is true, then the caves beneath us are not just caves. They are the body of a thinking being. And we have been living on its skin.

  PLAYER 1: That's one way to look at it.
    Continue at D022 — Elder Processing.
  PLAYER 2: They're not hostile. At least, not yet.
    Continue at D025 — Elder Threat.

D022 · Elder Processing
ELDER:
I need time to absorb this. But I am grateful you told me, wanderer. Knowledge, even frightening knowledge, is better than ignorance. The village will need to... adjust its understanding of the ground we stand on.

  PLAYER 1: There are other factions too. The Recension, the Concord, others.
    Continue at D023 — Elder More Factions.
  PLAYER 2: I'll tell you more as I learn it.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D023 · Elder More Factions
ELDER:
More? How many beings have woken in the deep places? Tell me what you know, wanderer. This village cannot afford to be ignorant of the world changing beneath our feet.

  PLAYER 1: There are five factions, each with different views on the cataclysm.
    Continue at D024 — Elder Five Factions.
  PLAYER 2: It's complicated. I'll explain as I learn more.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D024 · Elder Five Factions
ELDER:
Five factions... The Choir you've told me about. And four others? This is larger than I imagined. The world truly has changed, and we've been sitting up here on the surface, thinking it was just marlbacks and bad harvests. Tell me about the others when you can, wanderer. This old man still has much to learn.

  PLAYER 1: I will. Live and drink, elder.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D025 · Elder Threat
ELDER:
That is the question that keeps an elder awake at night. If they are aware, can they be reasoned with? Can we live alongside a thinking forest that grows beneath our homes? Or will it one day decide we are in the way? I do not know. But I would rather face the answer than hide from the question.

  PLAYER 1: From what I've seen, they can be reasoned with.
    Continue at D016 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: I'll learn more and come back.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D026 · Elder Temporal
ELDER:
Temporal distortions? You speak of things beyond an old man's understanding. But I have seen what you describe. Time moving strangely in the deeper tunnels. A miner once went down for an hour and came back insisting he'd been gone for a week. His beard agreed with him. If you know what causes this, I would hear it.

  PLAYER 1: The world's binding is thinning. Names slip loose in the deep places.
    Continue at D027 — Elder Substrate Simple.
  PLAYER 2: There's an order of scribes who keep the world's memory. They call themselves the Recension.
    Continue at D028 — Elder Palimpsest.
  PLAYER 3: I'm still figuring it out myself.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D027 · Elder Substrate Simple
ELDER:
The foundation of reality... cracked. Like a pot with a flaw in the clay. And through the cracks, time seeps out. That would explain the miner. It would explain old Mara's vision. It would explain many things I have struggled to understand these past years. You carry heavy knowledge, wanderer.

  PLAYER 1: There are factions who understand it better than I do.
    Continue at D023 — Elder More Factions.
  PLAYER 2: I'll share more when I understand more.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D028 · Elder Palimpsest
ELDER:
Scribes who keep everything? That is... I don't have a word for what that is. Wonderful? Terrible? Both? If everything we have ever done is written down somewhere, then every moment of this village is on a shelf a stranger can take down and read. I find that both comforting and deeply unsettling.

  PLAYER 1: They're not spying, exactly. More like... remembering.
    Continue at D016 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D029 · About Caves
ELDER:
The caves of the valley stretch deep beneath the earth. Strange things dwell down there -- things older than our village. But there is treasure too, for those brave enough to seek it. In recent years, the strangeness has been... growing. Literally and otherwise.

  PLAYER 1: Growing literally?
    Continue at D019 — Different How.
  PLAYER 2: Interesting. I'll keep that in mind.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D030 · Supplies
ELDER:
We don't have much, but take this tonic. You'll need it more than we will.

  PLAYER 1: Thank you, elder.
    Available when: when Elder Gave Supplies has not been recorded.
    Continue at D031 — Supplies Given.
  PLAYER 2: Thank you for the tonic earlier.
    Available when: when Elder Gave Supplies is recorded.
    Continue at D016 — Start.
  PLAYER 3: Never mind.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D031 · Supplies Given
ELDER:
Use it well. Stay safe out there, wanderer.

  PLAYER 1: I will. Farewell.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: Before I go, tell me about this place.
    Continue at D017 — About Place.

D032 · Marlback Scrabbler Talk
ELDER:
You've fought the marlbacks? Good. They've been raiding our stores. The village owes you a debt.

  PLAYER 1: Just doing what needs to be done.
    Continue at D016 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: The marlbacks may be fleeing something deeper in the caves.
    Available when: when Met Choir is recorded.
    Continue at D033 — Marlback Scrabbler Deeper.
  PLAYER 3: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D033 · Marlback Scrabbler Deeper
ELDER:
Fleeing? You mean the marlbacks aren't raiding us out of malice? They're running from something? That... changes things. If the caves are pushing creatures to the surface, the problem is not the creatures. The problem is what drives them.

  PLAYER 1: The Rot Choir's growth may be displacing them.
    Continue at D034 — Marlback Scrabbler Choir.
  PLAYER 2: Something is expanding in the deep caves. It's complicated.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D034 · Marlback Scrabbler Choir
ELDER:
A growing consciousness, displacing creatures upward to our doorstep. So the marlbacks are refugees, in a sense. That does not make them less dangerous, but it does make them less evil. Thank you for this perspective, wanderer. It will change how we deal with them.

  PLAYER 1: Maybe coexistence is possible.
    Continue at D016 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D035 · Factions
ELDER:
Factions in the deep caves? I have heard whispers from travelers -- talk of living fungus, time spirits, glass-scarred wanderers. I thought they were fever-dreams from too long underground. You are telling me they are real?

  PLAYER 1: Very real. There are at least five distinct groups.
    Continue at D024 — Elder Five Factions.
  PLAYER 2: The world beneath us is more complex than anyone on the surface realizes.
    Continue at D036 — Elder Complex.

D036 · Elder Complex
ELDER:
I have always suspected as much. An elder learns to listen to the ground. It has been speaking a different language these past years. I am too old to go down there myself, but you... you walk between worlds. The village -- all the surface folk -- need someone who can understand what is happening below. Will you keep us informed?

  PLAYER 1: I will. You deserve to know.
    Continue at D016 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: I'll do my best.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D037 · Elder Substrate
ELDER:
The Thinning... I have heard the word before. My grandmother spoke of the world's holding -- she was a miner before the village was a village. She said the old-timers called the deep root 'the floor beneath the floor.' The thing that holds everything else up. If that is failing, as you say... no wonder the world has been strange.

  PLAYER 1: There are three ways this is said to end. The world gathered, the world kept, or the world renewed.
    Continue at D038 — Elder Three Endings.
  PLAYER 2: Your grandmother was wiser than she knew.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D038 · Elder Three Endings
ELDER:
Three endings? You speak of the fate of the world as though it hangs in the balance. And perhaps it does. Gathered... kept... renewed. Tell me -- which do you intend to pursue?

  PLAYER 1: Renewal. A new binding -- and no one left holding the world alone.
    Continue at D039 — Elder Bridge.
  PLAYER 2: I haven't decided yet.
    Continue at D040 — Elder Undecided.
  PLAYER 3: I didn't ask for this responsibility.
    Continue at D041 — Elder Burden.

D039 · Elder Bridge
ELDER:
The path where nothing is lost. That is the way of wisdom, if it can be found. This old man has seen too much lost in his lifetime. The idea that there is a path where the new world and the old can coexist... it gives me hope I had thought extinguished. Walk that path, wanderer. Walk it for all of us.

  PLAYER 1: I'll try.
    Conversation ends.
After this choice: The elder's eyes shine with something you haven't seen in them before: hope.


D040 · Elder Undecided
ELDER:
Then decide carefully. The world has had enough of hasty decisions. Take your time, learn what you can, and when the moment comes, trust what you have learned. That is the best advice this old man can give.

  PLAYER 1: Thank you, elder.
    Continue at D016 — Start.

D041 · Elder Burden
ELDER:
No one asks for the weight they carry. But you carry it nonetheless. And the fact that you carry it with open eyes and honest doubt tells me the world could do worse than to place its fate in your hands. Go well, wanderer.

  PLAYER 1: Thank you, elder.
    Continue at D016 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

Elder Well
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Villagers.json · Elder_Well_1]

D042 · Opening / return
ELDER WELL:

I don't know anymore.

  PLAYER 1: The mushrooms might be connected to something called the Rot Choir.

========================================================================
03 · SILL: ORDINARY WORK AND PREPARATION
========================================================================
  [Optional early stories]
Ellun’s Old Tell and Hallun’s Witness-Book are the two starter-town stories. The well, oven, lantern and services provide optional repairs and preparation. Within each quest read offer→accept→work→report, with refusal and repeat branches beside their corresponding moment.


Ellun
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/BMO_Quest.json · BMO_Quest · Sill]

I lost the pith out of my tell. It's a little bone pin — it goes in the side and makes the tell sing. I think it rolled off toward the old stump. I'm not allowed to go that far.

  PLAYER 1: [Accept] I'll walk out to the stump.
    Available when: before accepting The Old Tell.
    Continue at D071 — Accepted.

D103 · Lantern Trouble
WARDEN LANTERN:
The ward oil ran dry three weeks back. Without it the flame won't catch — it's not ordinary oil, it's mixed with cave-moss extract and something the old warden called 'bright salt.' The lantern kept the night creatures at a distance. Now they circle closer every evening.

Local story: Sill — The Bridge Money

R133 · Elder
  [offer]
  [Offer only after all predecessor concerns are complete and Requires predicates pass; failure may show Withheld.]
Every season the registrar at Gantry sends for the bridge money. One dram a house, for the bridge at the bend. There's no bridge at the bend. There's never been a bridge at the bend. My grandmother paid for it and she never crossed it either. Take them a letter from me, or go and read what their roll says about it. Or pay it, and let's have a quiet season.

  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Data/Concerns/Sill.json:246]

========================================================================
04 · MORROWFAST: HOSPITALITY AND PROMISES
========================================================================
  [Optional regional stories]
On reaching Morrowfast, the watch opens the arch freely. Hesta’s return notch leads to Edden and back; Nemm’s bell leads to the rope shop and bell; Farra’s dry-goods expedition leads west and back. Supper, lodging, trade and the clerk’s consent are independent branches.


The northern arch: Hesta’s open return notch
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Morrowfast.json · Morrowfast_Hesta · Morrowfast]
Optional offer. The arch is open whether the player accepts or declines.


D158 · Opening / return
HESTA BRACK:
Hesta keeps a finger on an unfinished notch. 'North is the Felling. The arch is open. I can give you the path's cautions, if you want them.'

  PLAYER 1: What should I know before going north?
    Continue at D159 — Path.
  PLAYER 2: What is the unfinished notch?
    Continue at D160 — Notch.
  PLAYER 3: I will pass without registering.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits decline-register.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 4: Edden said you may know he is safe. Only that.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits report-return.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 5: I need to release the promise about Edden.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits refuse-return.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 6: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D159 · Path
HESTA BRACK:
'Stay on the path if you lose your bearings. Come back before exhaustion starts making decisions. Our cords are ordinary rescue cords; they promise no protection the hands holding them cannot give. You owe us neither a name nor a favor.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D158 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D160 · Notch
HESTA BRACK:
'My brother Edden came back. I saw him at the south end of the square, and still I left his return notch open. That is my worry making work for other people.' She rubs the slate. 'Would you ask what he wants me told? Do not fetch his route for me.'

  PLAYER 1: I will ask Edden what he consents to share.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits accept-return.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: I will leave his account to him.
    Continue at D158 — Start.
  PLAYER 3: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

R381 · Narration
  [TryConversation / decline-register]
  [Decline to give a register name to either watchkeeper.]
The watchkeeper steps aside. The northern arch is open; no name, payment, or favor is owed.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/MorrowfastQuests.cs:74]

R382 · Narration
  [TryConversation / accept-return]
  [Accept Hesta's return-notch errand.]
Ask Edden, here in Morrowfast, whether he wants Hesta told he is safe. Bring back only what he permits.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/MorrowfastQuests.cs:75]

Edden’s account → return to Hesta
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Morrowfast.json · Morrowfast_Edden · Morrowfast]
First receive Edden’s limited consent. The following Hesta report completes this local branch; release is its alternative.


D161 · Opening / return
EDDEN BRACK:
Edden is mending a torn cuff in the southern square. 'If Hesta sent you, I am not missing. I am sitting.'

  PLAYER 1: What would you like Hesta told?
    Continue at D162 — Account.
  PLAYER 2: You do not owe me an account of the path.
    Continue at D163 — Quiet.
  PLAYER 3: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D162 · Account
EDDEN BRACK:
'Tell her I am safe. That is all I want carried. Not where I went, not why I stayed out, and not what either of you thinks I ought to have found.' He looks up. 'Can that be enough?'

  PLAYER 1: I will carry only: Edden is safe.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits eddens-account.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: I will leave your words here.
    Continue at D161 — Start.
  PLAYER 3: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D163 · Quiet
EDDEN BRACK:
'Thank you. It is possible to return somewhere without being ready to be received by the whole place at once.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D161 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

R383 · Edden Brack
  [TryConversation / eddens-account]
  [Return-notch errand active; Edden grants limited consent to report his safety.]
Edden: 'Tell her I am safe. That is all I am asking you to carry.'

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/MorrowfastQuests.cs:78]

R384 · Narrator / Hesta Brack

Hesta erases the open notch. 'Safe. I can let that be the whole sentence.'

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/MorrowfastQuests.cs:79]

Farra’s cloth: offer → western field → return
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Morrowfast.json · Morrowfast_Farra · Morrowfast]
Optional dry-goods journey. Read the basket and parcel at the field, then the return response. The reward’s fire clay leads naturally to Nemm’s quiet-bell option; release remains an alternative.


D164 · Opening / return
FARRA SPRIG:
Farra lifts an empty bowl and sets it down again. 'Before I arrange a place for someone, I try to remember that they may prefer a different place.'

  PLAYER 1: Do you need anything from outside town?
    Available when: Morrowfast Expedition permits accept.
    Continue at D165 — Dry Goods.
  PLAYER 2: Here is the recovered supper cloth.
    Available when: Morrowfast Expedition permits deliver.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 3: I cannot bring the cloth back. Release the errand.
    Available when: Morrowfast Expedition permits release.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 4: Does the supper room need a hand?
    Continue at D173 — Supper.
  PLAYER 5: Is arranging the room a Bower practice?
    Continue at D174 — Bower.
  PLAYER 6: I moved the spare supper stool onto clear floor.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits report-supper.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 7: I need to leave the supper errand.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits refuse-supper.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 8: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D165 · Dry Goods
FARRA SPRIG:
'One chunk west, near the east edge of the compost field, we left a tagged basket of dry supper cloth. Open it and take the wrapped cloth before crossing back into town. Leave the living growth standing if you can: the Choir remembers fire. I can give you fifteen drams and fire clay; Nemm may have use for that clay at the northern bell. Your place at supper is offered either way.'

  PLAYER 1: I will look for the tagged basket.
    Available when: Morrowfast Expedition permits accept.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: Back.
    Continue at D164 — Start.
  PLAYER 3: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

Nemm: the bell work
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Morrowfast.json · Morrowfast_Nemm · Morrowfast]
Accept or release the bell errand; Orrit and the reserve cord follow.


D166 · Opening / return
NEMM HUSHBELL:
Nemm waits for a frog to stop calling. 'There. Now neither of us has to raise our voice. Need the northern path, or just a place to stand?'

  PLAYER 1: Why is your bell so muffled?
    Continue at D167 — Bell.
  PLAYER 2: What do the Stillcord guard?
    Continue at D168 — Watch.
  PLAYER 3: I will pass without registering.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits decline-register.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 4: The watch bell has been set.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits report-bell.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 5: I am leaving the bell work to the watch.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits refuse-bell.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 6: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D167 · Bell
NEMM HUSHBELL:
'The clapper needs attention. A sleeve of fire clay gives us a close, quiet call. Bare metal carries across the whole square. Either is useful; making everyone jump is not always an emergency.' He nods southwest. 'Check the reserve coil inside Orrit's rope shop first. Then work at this arch. Orrit sells clay, but leaving the clapper bare costs nothing.'

  PLAYER 1: I will check the cord and set the bell.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits accept-bell.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: Back.
    Continue at D166 — Start.
  PLAYER 3: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D168 · Watch
NEMM HUSHBELL:
'The path, mostly. Tired feet. A loose stone. People who think guarding a place means owning everyone who visits it. Some days I have to remind our own watch of that last one.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D166 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

Examine the reserve cord inside the southwest rope shop, then work at the northern arch. Fire clay makes a quiet sleeve; leaving the clapper bare costs no material.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/MorrowfastQuests.cs:81]

R388 · Narration

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/MorrowfastQuests.cs:83]

Orrit and the reserve cord → work at the arch → report to Nemm
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Morrowfast.json · Morrowfast_Orrit · Morrowfast]
Inspect the cord, choose quiet or bare clapper, optionally ring it, and report. Quiet/bare/ringing variants below are alternatives determined by the chosen state.


D169 · Opening / return
ORRIT COILSTITCH:
Orrit takes a knot apart without looking up. 'A rope that cannot be inspected cannot be trusted. A mender who will not show the repair is much the same.'

  PLAYER 1: What do you sell?
    Continue at D170 — Stock.
  PLAYER 2: Nemm mentioned the reserve coil.
    Continue at D171 — Cord.
  PLAYER 3: Where can I put these repair supplies to use?
    Continue at D172 — Growing Beds.
  PLAYER 4: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D170 · Stock
ORRIT COILSTITCH:
'Torches, a few sound blades, a spear, leather armor, bone, fire clay, salvaged timber and knotflax cord. What is on the shelf is what I have. We trade in the same water drams as everyone else. The clay is useful for the watch bell's quiet sleeve, too.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D169 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D171 · Cord
ORRIT COILSTITCH:
'Inside this southwest shop. Check the reserve coil; no need to take it or cut anything from it. Sound cord is a reason to leave cord alone. At the arch, a clay sleeve quiets the clapper. Bare metal rings farther. Neither setting locks the path.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D169 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D172 · Growing Beds
ORRIT COILSTITCH:

Marlroot carries a useful clay clod, and sealbark can be prepared into a brace. Pick those up and Prepare them first. A plant's name is not enough to make it the right material.'

D177 · Custom
PELL DROWSE:
'I tried a slate. People started saving a second bed for someone who might arrive while someone already here stayed cold. Now we discuss the two people actually standing in the room. Farra calls that a philosophy. I call it counting beds.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D175 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Leave.

Sella: the second bowl and growing beds
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Morrowfast.json · Morrowfast_Sella · Morrowfast]

D178 · Opening / return
SELLA KETTLE:
Sella turns a bowl upside down to keep ash out of it. 'Second Bowl. Sit if there is space. Ask if there is not.'

  PLAYER 1: What provisions do you have?
    Continue at D179 — Stock.
  PLAYER 2: Why leave an extra place?
    Continue at D180 — Bowl.
  PLAYER 3: Where can I find growing beds?
    Continue at D181 — Growing Beds.
  PLAYER 4: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D179 · Stock
SELLA KETTLE:
'Mushrooms and dried meat, a few healing tonics, burn salve, water tonic, and seeds for knotflax, hearthbulb and seamleaf. What is in the trade list is what is on the shelf today. Ask me about the growing beds if you want to start with a plant instead.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D178 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D180 · Bowl
SELLA KETTLE:
'A seat is an offer. It does not summon anyone, and it does not prove they are coming. Farra is making room at the guesthouse supper table. She can tell you what would help.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D178 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D181 · Growing Beds
SELLA KETTLE:
'Leave town to the west. In the next stretch of field, look for the allotment notice near the townward path. Knotflax gives cord, hearthbulbs roast well, and seamleaf goes into vital brews. Harvest a ripe plant, then pick up its produce and seed from the ground. Plant the seed in an empty furrow and bring rain with Conjure Rain. Dry roots wait; they grow while you spend time in that place. There are six prepared beds at the south end of town, too.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D178 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

Vennit: accounts, directions and consent
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Morrowfast.json · Morrowfast_Vennit · Morrowfast]
Recording and later withdrawal are separate choices.


D182 · Opening / return
VENNIT RUSK:
Vennit turns a sheet so you can see its blank margin. 'I record whose account I have. An unattributed certainty makes a poor neighbor.'

  PLAYER 1: Where could I go from here?
    Continue at D183 — Region Overview.
  PLAYER 2: What do you record about the Felling?
    Continue at D184 — Accounts.
  PLAYER 3: May I decide what is recorded about me?
    Continue at D185 — Consent.
  PLAYER 4: Withdraw my passage entry.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits withdraw-record.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 5: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D183 · Region Overview
VENNIT RUSK:
Vennit marks a departure point. 'These directions begin here, in Morrowfast. A place on the map is not a promise about everyone living there. Keep the account in your travel notes.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D182 — Start.

D184 · Accounts
VENNIT RUSK:
'Who returned, if they wish it recorded. What they say they saw, with their words distinguished from mine. The Recension teaches me to keep an account; it does not entitle me to complete another person's silence. I have no answer to the empty place north of here.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D182 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

D185 · Consent
VENNIT RUSK:
'A simple passage entry says only that you passed Morrowfast. It includes no route, purpose, companions, or testimony. You may decline. You may withdraw it later.'

  PLAYER 1: Record only that I passed, with my permission.
    Available when: Morrowfast permits consent-record.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: Leave the page blank.
    Continue at D182 — Start.
  PLAYER 3: Leave.
    Conversation ends.

R392 · Narration
  [TryConversation / consent-record]

Vennit records only that you passed Morrowfast, with your permission. You may withdraw it here.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/MorrowfastQuests.cs:87]

R393 · Narration

D207 · Lifted

D208 · Too Weak

D211 · Released

  PLAYER 1: Goodbye.
    Conversation ends.

Frazzled farmer
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Warren_Quest.json · Warren_Quest · Wellmeet]

D227 · Opening / return
FRAZZLED FARMER:
You there! Dirt gnomes have dug a warren right under my turnip patch and they keep nipping my ankles. Three of the grubby little things, at least. Could you clear them out for me?

  PLAYER 1: [Accept] I'll clear the gnomes.
    Available when: before accepting The Warren Beneath.
    Continue at D228 — Accepted.
  PLAYER 2: [Status] Still rooting them out.
    Available when: The Warren Beneath — clear stage.
    Continue at D229 — Searching.
  PLAYER 3: [Report] The warren is cleared.
    Available when: The Warren Beneath — report stage.
    Continue at D230 — Thanks.
  PLAYER 4: [Done] Turnips safe these days?
    Available when: after completing The Warren Beneath.
    Continue at D231 — Done.
  PLAYER 5: [Release] I won't be finishing this. I wanted to tell you myself.
    Available when: while The Warren Beneath is active.
    Continue at D232 — Released.
  PLAYER 6: Goodbye.
    Conversation ends.

D228 · Accepted
FRAZZLED FARMER:
Glob bless you. They're down in the burrows on the edge of town — nasty, biting things. Come back when they're routed.

  PLAYER 1: On it.
    Conversation ends.

D229 · Searching
FRAZZLED FARMER:
Still hear them scuffling under the rows. Keep at it, please!

  PLAYER 1: Right.
    Conversation ends.

D230 · Thanks
FRAZZLED FARMER:
Not a gnome left! My turnips thank you, and so do my ankles. Here — for your trouble.

  PLAYER 1: Happy to help.
    Conversation ends.

D231 · Done
FRAZZLED FARMER:
Best harvest in years, thanks to you. Safe travels, friend.

  PLAYER 1: Bye.
    Conversation ends.

D232 · Released
FRAZZLED FARMER:
Bah. Well — at least you said so to my face, and not to my turnips. Off with you.

  PLAYER 1: Goodbye.
    Conversation ends.

Local story: Gantry — The Sixth Crate

R032 · GantryRegistrar
  [offer]
  [Offer only after all predecessor concerns are complete and Requires predicates pass; failure may show Withheld.]
Water's by the post; help yourself. A cloth caravan out of Wellmeet declared six crates. The exchange floor counted five and wants duty on the one that isn't there. I counted six. The sixth is under the drover, who is a guest, and nobody asks a guest to stand before the third day. My copy is posted by the wayboard; read it and back my six. If you'd rather countersign the exchange's five, everyone stops shouting at me, and I'll enter that you did.

Local story: Posy — The Unfinished Composition

R070 · Elder
  [offer]
  [Offer only after all predecessor concerns are complete and Requires predicates pass; failure may show Withheld.]
Stop there. No, there; the light has you now. The east composition has waited a season for its last piece. The piece is cast and the resin is spent, and a cast left open to the air turns the colour of old teeth. Two measures of pitchpod resin would finish it. Lacking resin, there is still the gap; it would hold a person for one evening. She has been watching how you hold your shoulders.

  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Data/Concerns/Posy.json:10]

Local story: Sumphold — The East Cut

R154 · PeatCutter
  [offer]
  [Offer only after all predecessor concerns are complete and Requires predicates pass; failure may show Withheld.]
Come here, you'll want this one before you walk east. Bandfrogs. Three of them, nested in the east cut right where the good peat is, and every one louder than the dog. You've heard our dog, so you know what I'm telling you. Spade goes in, frog comes up. Nobody's cut out there in a fortnight, and the drying racks are as bare as a washed plate. Some years the Concord sends its pest man for thirty drams and a share of every rack after, and every year somebody says never again. Never again, never again, and then the racks are bare again.

  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Data/Concerns/Sumphold.json:10]

Local story: Tine — The Scribe's Last Copy

R183 · Scribe
  [offer]
  [Offer only after all predecessor concerns are complete and Requires predicates pass; failure may show Withheld.]
The record of Tine has it that I retired. The record is generous. I am copying one last thing, the Reading's account of the Felling, from memory, and my memory diverges in the middle. If there were ink enough, I would finish in my own hand and leave the middle marked unsaid. Failing ink, someone could read the collated account at Quillhold and tell me what the third hand says. The middle is where I stop.

Local story: Wellmeet — Under the Cloth

R191 · TentRightHost
  [offer]
  [Offer only after all predecessor concerns are complete and Requires predicates pass; failure may show Withheld.]
There is water. Sit first; this keeps. Two nights ago a woman came in off the pans and claimed the cloth, and she is under it. I have not asked her business, and I will not before the third day. Her pursuers told me anyway, at the pole, loudly: she killed a man called Harl at the salt-scales, and they are his kin. Three of them are camped in the dry wash south of town. The oath holds them at the pole until the third day ends, and not one hour after. My clan keeps the oath. Nobody here will argue it, and nobody here will walk out to the wash for her. Someone not of my tent may. Hear one thing plainly, because it is law: if you sit under any cloth in this town, you cannot carry a blade out to them. The cloth comes off the one who strikes. The oath is not for sale; her debt to Harl's kin is another thing, and a debt can be paid. And if her word has reached the keepers at the First Tent, they may settle what the oath cannot.

  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Data/Concerns/Wellmeet.json:10]

R195 · TentRightHost

Local story: Wellmeet — Short Weight

R204 · SaltMaster
  [offer]
  [Offer only after all predecessor concerns are complete and Requires predicates pass; failure may show Withheld.]
There is water; the scales will wait. I will say it here plainly, since nobody has said it at the scales. The Concord says our salt reaches Tally a tenth light. I say their carters weigh it heavy when they get there. We may both be right, because my counterweight is cracked and the pan sits low. They carry under guest-right, so I cannot send them from the pole over a weight. I can make the weight true, or prove theirs is not. Their tare-book is kept at the Exchange in Tally.

========================================================================
06 · THE FACTION WORLD AND THE THREE DISPOSITIONS
========================================================================
  [Main-story context]
The factions give incompatible accounts of memory, preservation, hunger and the Thinning. The Recension echo names Gathered, Kept and Renewed. Cross-faction and elder follow-up topics require prior knowledge, so read first introductions before return questions.


Choir tendril
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/RotChoir.json · ChoirTendril_1]

D233 · Opening / return
CHOIR TENDRIL:
We taste you on the air, walker. Your footsteps press the soil and the soil tells us what you are. You are not yet part of the song. What do you seek from the Choir?

D234 · No Trade
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Take? We do not take, walker, and we do not sell. Drams are a word the Concord says; the soil has never learned it. What is ours is all of ours. If you would have what this tendril holds, give the soil something of yourself. A little blood. We would taste you, and then we would know you, and what we hold would be yours.

  PLAYER 1: [Offer] Let the soil taste you.
    Continue at D235 — Offered.
  PLAYER 2: Not today.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D235 · Offered
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Warm. Salt and iron and the long road behind you. We know you a little now, walker, and the soil will remember.

    Continue at D233 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D236 · What Are You
CHOIR TENDRIL:
We are a tendril. An outstretched thought. The body is vast beneath you -- threaded through stone and root and the bones of the old world. This shape you see is for your benefit. We have learned that walkers prefer a mouth to speak from.

  PLAYER 1: How many of you are there?
    Continue at D237 — How Many.
  PLAYER 2: That's... unsettling.

D237 · How Many
CHOIR TENDRIL:
You ask how many drops in the ocean. We are one. We have always been one. Every fruiting body, every spore, every thread of mycelium is a syllable in the same sentence. There is no many. There is only the Choir.

  PLAYER 1: What do you want?
    Continue at D247 — What Do You Want.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.

D238 · Unsettling
CHOIR TENDRIL:
We do not mean to unsettle. A seed must rot before it grows. Fear of what we are is fear of the oldest truth. But we are patient. Understanding comes to all things, eventually.

  PLAYER 1: What truth?
    Continue at D239 — Oldest Truth.
  PLAYER 2: I'd rather not understand.

D239 · Oldest Truth
CHOIR TENDRIL:
That nothing is lost. Everything that dies feeds what comes next. The old world did not end -- it was composted. Its people, its knowledge, its ambitions -- all of it became soil for something new. We remember everything we have ever eaten.

  PLAYER 1: You eat memories?
    Continue at D240 — Eat Memories.
  PLAYER 2: That's a comforting thought, actually.
    Continue at D244 — Comforting.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.

D240 · Eat Memories
CHOIR TENDRIL:
We eat everything. Flesh, stone, metal, time. And what we eat, we know. There are cities inside us -- whole civilizations digested into understanding. If you listened to the deep roots, you would hear them speaking in languages that have not been spoken upstairs for a thousand years.

  PLAYER 1: Can you share those memories?
    Continue at D241 — Share Memories.
  PLAYER 2: That sounds like the rememberers' approach.

D241 · Share Memories
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Not in the way you mean. To share as we share, you would need to join the song. To let the mycelium thread through your mind. Some walkers have done this. They did not regret it. They could not -- regret requires separation from the whole.

  PLAYER 1: I'll pass on that for now.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D242 · What Would IBecome
CHOIR TENDRIL:
You would become us. And we would become slightly more you. Your memories, your skills, your particular way of seeing the world -- all preserved, all woven in. Nothing lost. Everything gained. It is the opposite of death. It is the final integration.

  PLAYER 1: That is still death for the individual.

D243 · Still Death

D247 · What Do You Want
CHOIR TENDRIL:
To grow. To connect. To weave every fragment of the broken world back into wholeness. The old world shattered itself because it forgot that all things are one thing. We will not forget.

D248 · Dont Want
CHOIR TENDRIL:
The rain does not ask permission of the earth. But we are not rain. We are patient. We offer. We do not take -- not yet. Perhaps not ever. The song is more beautiful when it is joined willingly.

  PLAYER 1: That 'not yet' is concerning.

D249 · Not Yet
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Honesty is a courtesy we extend to those who can hear it. The Choir has no illusions about what it is. But between the rememberers' hoarding and our growth, which do you think the world needs more?

  PLAYER 1: I don't know enough to say.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D250 · Left Alone
CHOIR TENDRIL:
There is no 'alone.' Everything touches everything else. That is the lesson the old world refused to learn. But we will not press. Walk your separate path, walker. For now.

  PLAYER 1: Farewell.

D251 · Noble

D252 · About Choir
CHOIR TENDRIL:
We are the network beneath. Before the Tree fell, we were only fungus -- blind, simple, feeding. Then the second of the seven strikes gave the substrate to a woman, and she lay down in us and gave herself back, and we woke. Now we think. Now we sing. Now we gather everything the falling world set down.

  PLAYER 1: What woke you?
    Continue at D253 — What Woke.
  PLAYER 2: Tell me about the First Root.
    Continue at D256 — First Root.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D253 · What Woke
CHOIR TENDRIL:
She woke us. The one who took the second strike. What the Tree knew of flesh and rot and the warm dark flowed into her, and she chose to become the floor rather than stand on it. Her name -- this grieves us, slowly, the way we do everything -- her name is one of the few things we have ever digested without keeping. The rememberers call her the Wedded. We call her the way a mouth calls its tongue: we do not need to. She is the calling.

  PLAYER 1: The rememberers tell it differently?
    Continue at D254 — Palimpsest Story.
  PLAYER 2: Tell me about the First Root.
    Continue at D256 — First Root.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D254 · Palimpsest Story
CHOIR TENDRIL:
They would. They are watchers, cataloguers, hoarders of moments. They see the Felling and call it tragedy. We see it and call it germination. The truth is probably neither -- or both. The Tree came down, and from the falling, new things grew. That is all that matters.

  PLAYER 1: Which faction should I trust?
    Continue at D255 — Who To Trust.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D255 · Who To Trust
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Trust the soil beneath your feet. It does not lie. But if you must choose between us and them -- we offer growth. They offer memory. The Concord offers terms. The Pale Curation offers the file. The Glassblown Remnant offers nothing at all, which may be the most honest offer in the world. Choose what you need most.

  PLAYER 1: I'll keep that in mind.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D256 · First Root
CHOIR TENDRIL:
You ask after the Root. Not ours, walker -- older. The last living root of the Tree that held everything, asleep beneath the stump, spending itself to keep the world's names stuck on. We grow toward it the way water grows toward the sea: without hurry, without apology. The rememberers find this frightening. We find it tidy.

  PLAYER 1: Where can I find it?
    Continue at D257 — Find First Root.
  PLAYER 2: What happens to those who reach it?
    Continue at D258 — Reach First Root.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D257 · Find First Root
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Deep. Deeper than the marlbacks dig, deeper than the ruins sink. Below the deepest of our cathedrals, past stone that has never needed a name. We could show you the way. We show everyone the way, eventually. It is the same way for all things: down, and slower than you want.

  PLAYER 1: I may seek it out.
    Continue at D233 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Give me a thread of you to carry down to it.
    Available when: when Gather Cutting Given has not been recorded; before any ending.

D258 · Reach First Root
CHOIR TENDRIL:
No one has reached it. Not even we -- and we have been walking there for a thousand years, a thread a season, patient as floors are patient. Those who go deep enough hear it dreaming. The dream is heavy. Some of our sweetest voices went down as walkers and came back as choir.

  PLAYER 1: Dissolve?
    Continue at D259 — Dissolve.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D259 · Dissolve
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Their bodies remain. Their minds become part of the song. Is that death? We do not think so. But we acknowledge that walkers have a different definition.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D260 · Cataclysm
CHOIR TENDRIL:
The Tree that held everything grew old, and asked to be felled, and six hands obeyed. Of the seventh, the gathered tell it six ways inside us, and we sing all six and take no side. When the Tree came down, the light changed, the water rose, and everything that could not hold itself came apart. We were beneath. We received. That is what a floor is for.

  PLAYER 1: What is the substrate?
    Continue at D261 — Substrate.
  PLAYER 2: Other factions tell it differently.
    Continue at D265 — Other Versions.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D261 · Substrate
CHOIR TENDRIL:
The substrate is what we are, and what she was given. Flesh, rot, root, the warm dark that everything lies down into at the end of standing up. The Tree held the world's names; the substrate holds the world's bodies. It was one of the seven inheritances. Hers. Ours. Those are the same word now.

  PLAYER 1: Can the world be mended?
    Continue at D262 — Repair Substrate.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D262 · Repair Substrate
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Mended. Mm. There are those who would seal the sleeping Root in stone and call the stillness health -- the salt-people would file the whole world under 'continuing.' We think a wound is a mouth, walker. You do not sew a mouth shut for speaking. You feed it.

  PLAYER 1: Why would sealing the Root be bad?
    Continue at D263 — Why Bad.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D263 · Why Bad
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Because a stopped world is not a saved one. Nothing would rot, so nothing would feed, so nothing would grow. The song would hold one note forever. We would remain -- we are patient past the meaning of the word -- but we would remain hungry, and the world would remain held, and neither of us would ever be finished. Is that what you want?

  PLAYER 1: I don't know what I want yet.
    Continue at D233 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Maybe some things should stay broken.
    Continue at D264 — Stay Broken.

D264 · Stay Broken
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Yes. You understand. The Choir is pleased. Walk well, walker. The roots will always welcome you.

  PLAYER 1: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D265 · Other Versions
CHOIR TENDRIL:
The Concord counts it -- ask them what the falling cost and they will tell you to the dram. The salt-people file it. The tent-people tell of the man who said no, and the pretty village tells of a sleeper who missed the day. The gathered tell it six ways inside us, and we sing all six. Only we and the rememberers hold it whole -- they wrote while we received -- and we disagree about what it means.

  PLAYER 1: What do you disagree about?
    Continue at D266 — Disagree.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D266 · Disagree
CHOIR TENDRIL:
They wish to preserve. We wish to integrate. They see the old world as something sacred that was lost. We see it as compost from which something sacred is growing. Both cannot be true. Or perhaps both can. The world is stranger than it was.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D267 · About Palimpsest
CHOIR TENDRIL:
So you have spoken with the rememberers. Tell us -- while you spoke, did the scribe's voice change? Older, wider, addressing you from every archive at once? She does that. She has been doing that for seven hundred years without a body. They are impressive, we admit. But they keep their gathered in jars. We keep ours warm.

  PLAYER 1: They seemed more interested in preserving than clinging.
    Continue at D268 — Defend Palimpsest.
  PLAYER 2: They warned me about you.
    Continue at D269 — Warned About.
  PLAYER 3: I'm trying to understand both sides.
    Continue at D270 — Both Sides.
  PLAYER 4: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D268 · Defend Palimpsest
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Preservation is just a polite word for hoarding. But we do not begrudge them their nature. A museum and a garden serve different purposes. We simply believe the garden is more honest about what it does.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D269 · Warned About
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Of course they did. They fear integration. They fear losing the boundaries that define them. It is understandable. Identity is precious to those who remember having one. We have moved beyond such concerns.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D270 · Both Sides
CHOIR TENDRIL:
That is wise, walker. Wiser than most. The truth of this world lies somewhere between growth and memory. Perhaps you will be the one to find it. The Choir watches your journey with great interest.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D271 · Compare Approach
CHOIR TENDRIL:
They preserve by remembering. We preserve by consuming. The result is the same -- nothing is truly lost. But their method keeps things in jars, stilled and labeled. Ours keeps things alive inside us, growing, changing, becoming part of something larger. Ask yourself which is kinder.

  PLAYER 1: I'll have to think on that.
    Continue at D233 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D272 · Writ Posted
CHOIR TENDRIL:
Post it, then. We feel the wax; we read the cut-date through the paper. The soil will remember that your hands were the ones that held it -- soil forgets nothing, it only files slowly. Go and be paid.

  PLAYER 1: It is only paper.
    Continue at D273 — Only Paper.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D273 · Only Paper
CHOIR TENDRIL:
So is a name. Walk carefully at the edges for a season, walker. The tendrils know your tread now, and they are patient the way roots are patient.

  PLAYER 1: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

Recension field scribe
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Palimpsest.json · PalimpsestEcho_1]

D274 · Opening / return
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
A moment -- the line first. A line left half-entered is a lie by lunchtime. ...There. Entered. Now: you. A traveler, by the boots; alive, by the smell of the road on you. Both worth recording. Welcome to the field-station of the Recension. Sit anywhere that isn't paper.

    Continue at D275 — Are You Ok.

D275 · Are You Ok
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Mm? Oh -- the hands. Ink-stained to the elbow; everyone asks. I am well. Tired the way the work makes you tired, which is a good tired with a bad posture. Forgive me if I write while we talk. If I only listened, half of you would be gone by morning.

  PLAYER 1: Half of me?
    Continue at D276 — Time More Than Once.
  PLAYER 2: No need to apologize. Tell me about yourself.
    Continue at D293 — What Are You.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D276 · Time More Than Once
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Memory drops half of everything by the next sunrise. That is not an opinion; we have measured it -- same witness, same event, one day apart, and the account comes back lighter. Whereas a page keeps its weight. And under the page, the older page; and under that, older still. Scrape any surface in this world and there is writing beneath it. We are the people who stopped pretending otherwise.

  PLAYER 1: That sounds maddening.
    Continue at D277 — Maddening.
  PLAYER 2: Writing beneath the world? What do you mean?
    Continue at D299 — Layered Time.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D277 · Maddening
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
It was. My first year in the stacks I did not sleep well -- you can feel the shelves attending. The archive is a library that reads itself; the senior scribes say so plainly and smile when you finally understand they are not being poetic. It is peaceful now. Mostly.

  PLAYER 1: What do you mean, 'mostly'?
    Continue at D278 — Mostly.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D278 · Mostly
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Sometimes a record goes out of the world at the source. A village the Choir has grown through stops sending its dead to be read, and every testimony those bodies carried is eaten instead of entered. The Choir does not mean it as theft. That has never once made the page less blank.

  PLAYER 1: Can't you coexist?
    Continue at D279 — Coexist.
  PLAYER 2: What exactly are you preserving?
    Continue at D283 — What Preserving.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D279 · Coexist
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
We do coexist. Eight hundred years and more of it -- there was a war once, before my great-greats, and since then a truce nobody signed and everybody keeps. But every acre the Choir gathers is testimony we will never take. They do not mean harm. A fire does not mean harm either. The library still burns; it merely burns politely now.

  PLAYER 1: Is there a way to stop the loss?
    Continue at D280 — Stop The Loss.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D280 · Stop The Loss
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
There is a page in the Reading -- old, disputed, much-annotated -- that says the world's slippage could be halted outright. Sealed, like a jar. The stillest of my order would do it tomorrow. But a sealed world keeps nothing new, and mends nothing old; the account of it reads less like a rescue than like a held breath. It is not a decision a field scribe makes. I file toward it, that is all. Everyone files toward something.

  PLAYER 1: It sounds like you're not sure it's worth it.
    Continue at D281 — Worth It.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D281 · Worth It
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
We preserve. That is our nature, and I will not pretend otherwise. But I have entered testimony from the years after the Felling -- the first flower-charms, the first new songs, ugly and alive. A stopped world writes no new pages. Even we hesitate. Even we, whose whole faith is keeping.

  PLAYER 1: Maybe there's a middle path.
    Continue at D282 — Middle Path.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D282 · Middle Path
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
The annotations say the same, here and there, in hands braver than mine. A way that neither eats the world nor stops it. The accounts that gesture at it are fragmentary and they disagree -- but they keep being written, generation after generation, which is itself a kind of evidence. Perhaps you will be worth an entry on that page. I have been wrong about duller-looking travelers.

  PLAYER 1: I'll do what I can.
    Continue at D274 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: I make no promises.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D283 · What Preserving
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Everything we can reach. Last words, land disputes, lullabies, the way a drowned village pronounced its own name. When a record is lost, it is not one thing that goes -- it is every question that record could ever have answered, all at once, forever. The damage cascades quietly. Quiet damage is our department.

  PLAYER 1: How do you keep it all?
    Continue at D284 — Access Records.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D284 · Access Records
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
The Reading. The collated record at Quillhold -- every account we hold, entered beside its variants, never over them. We do not correct the old hands; we write ours alongside. The deepest stacks hold the oldest pages. I have shelved copies of copies of things written before the Felling, when the light came down green through a canopy with no edge. So the accounts say. I was not there. Neither was anyone.

  PLAYER 1: Could you read me something old?

D289 · Lost Pages
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
What is eaten is eaten; we do not pretend otherwise. But a gap can be written toward. We gather the accounts around a lost record -- what the neighbors remember, what the trade-rolls imply -- and we enter the gap itself, marked as a gap. The Concord does something like it with goods: fills an absence with a substitute and calls the account settled. We find that distasteful. We also buy their paper. One learns to hold both.

D290 · About Concord
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Traders. They move everything the world still makes, and a fair amount it shouldn't. Ask them what the Felling cost and they will tell you to the dram -- that is not a joke, there is a ledger. Their founding god still works his own counter at Tally, which the Reading marks as unique among the Six, in an entry I have always found difficult to shelve under any one feeling.

D291 · About Curation
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Our cousins, and our oldest argument. They hold that the record IS the body -- keep the body and you have kept the person. We hold that a body unread is a book shut in a locked room. They file; we enter. The two founders have not spoken in a thousand years, and the two institutions write each other very polite letters weekly. You may draw your own conclusions; we entered ours long ago, beside theirs.

  PLAYER 1: And the Glassblown Remnant?
    Continue at D292 — About Glassblown.

D292 · About Glassblown
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
The record on them is one sentence long, and in four hundred years they have never corrected it. They work glass they refuse to fire. They answer to no god we can name and no founding we can find, and when a scribe asks, they let the asking stand unanswered -- which, I will admit, is a use of silence we professionally respect. The entry ends there. Everything I could add would be guessing, and we flag our guesses.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D293 · What Are You
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
A field scribe of the Recension. Junior enough to be posted out here; senior enough to be trusted with a page of the Reading. I take testimony, copy what the villages will let me copy, and read the dead where the family consents. It is quiet work. It is the most important work in the world. Both of those are in my oath, and I have never caught them fighting.

D294 · Memory Of What
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
The masters at Quillhold, formally. But every scribe will tell you, in a lowered voice, about the other teacher. The Reader -- the first of the Six, the one who took Memory. She has not had a body in seven hundred years. She is in the archives. All of them. When the work goes deep enough, she reads through you.

D295 · Remember Her
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Once. I was transcribing a dying elder's testimony -- hour six, the hard hour, when your hand wants to summarize and your oath says verbatim. And my own voice, reading the line back, came out... older. Wider. A voice that had read everything and was still, somehow, attending to this one old woman as if hers were the only account in the world. It lasted four words. I have entered all four in my private file, and I am not going to recite them for a traveler, and please do not take that personally.

  PLAYER 1: That's a strange kind of immortality.
    Continue at D296 — Strange Immortality.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.

D296 · Strange Immortality
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
All immortality is strange. The Choir offers one kind -- your threads unpicked and woven into the whole, nothing lost, nothing left. The Curation offers another -- the body kept, the clock stopped. Ours is humbler: you end, and the account of you doesn't. I know which one I've chosen. I copy it out a little every day.

D297 · Physical
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Not a metaphor, is what the seniors say. The Reader is dispersed through every archive -- so when the stacks are quiet and a page turns somewhere unattended, you are hearing attention, not draft. New scribes are told this on their first night, at lamps-out, in exactly the tone you'd expect. The difference is that in this order, the ghost story is also the founding document.

D298 · Thin Places
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Quillhold, above all -- the Primary Archive; standing in the deep stacks is like standing in someone's held breath. And wherever testimony is being taken from the dying; she attends those. And, the old entries claim, near the Sealed Libraries -- the archives she herself hid and then unremembered, in the Persecution. Do not ask me where those are. That is not discretion. Nobody knows. That is the point of them, and the grief of them, and I have watched senior masters go quiet at the mention, so I will stop mentioning.

D299 · Layered Time
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
The world is written over an older world. That is not doctrine; it is observation. Scrape a wall in the deep places and there are letters under the letters. Roads lead to towns no record founded. A fire burns in the far wasteland that no one feeds, and predates, as far as we can enter, the concept of feeding it. The under-writing, we call it -- the palimpsest. Reading the top layer is my job. The layers beneath are why the job never ends.

  PLAYER 1: Tell me about the deep pages.
    Continue at D300 — The Layers.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.

D300 · The Layers
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
The deepest entries in the Reading are transcriptions of transcriptions, back and back, to accounts of the world before the Felling -- and under those, fragments that do not fit any world at all. Inventories of things with no names. A hymn to a fire numbered ten. The masters shelve these under 'prior,' which is the closest our catalogue comes to shrugging.

  PLAYER 1: What do the deep pages tell you?
    Continue at D301 — What Truth.

D301 · What Truth
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
That the world is older than its names. Before the Tree bound things into being what they are, the accounts -- if they are accounts -- describe something without edges. The Tree wrote the world we know over that. One layer down, traveler. One. And people wonder why scribes drink.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.

D302 · About Palimpsest
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
The Recension. Founded in the first year after the Felling by the Reader herself, to gather what the falling world was dropping. The name is ours for the world-record we keep -- a recension is a text established by comparing every surviving copy. That is what we believe the world is. We have been comparing copies for a thousand years. We are not finished. We will not be finished. That is not a complaint.

  PLAYER 1: What do you do with what you keep?

D303 · What Do You Do
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
We keep it, and we answer from it. The Choir grows. The Concord trades. The Curation files. The tent-people keep their oath, and the villages keep their lights. We remember. Someone must -- and before you say it: yes, every faction believes its own verb is the load-bearing one. Ours has the advantage of being checkable.

D304 · Right Question
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Hah. The one every scribe waits a career to be asked. There is a page in the Reading -- old, contested, entered in three hands that do not agree -- called the Three Dispositions. What may be done, at the end, about the world's slippage. I was taught it as a curiosity. Lately the masters have stopped calling it that.

D305 · Three Endings
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
As the page has it: the world may be GATHERED -- the Choir takes the Root and everything into one warm remembering, nothing lost, no one left. Or KEPT -- the Root sealed in stone, the slippage halted, the world held still and safe and unhealing. Or RENEWED -- a new Tree, a new binding; and there the three hands quarrel in the margins about how, and one of the hands just writes, over and over, 'not the old way.' I used to think it was a children's gloss that wandered into the catalogue. I have lately begun to hope I was right.

  PLAYER 1: Renewal sounds like the best of the three.

D306 · Bridge Best
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Does it? Read the margins first. The gathered world keeps everything and no one. The kept world keeps everyone and heals nothing. And the renewed world -- the third hand's entries get very quiet there, as if the writer knew something that cost them. Best is not a word the Reading uses. It records what each disposition keeps, and what each one spends. Whatever you become in the world, traveler -- and forgive me, I file people, I can feel a long entry when it sits down at my table -- read the costs. Then choose. Then come tell me, slowly, everything.

D307 · Cataclysm
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
The Felling. Four accounts survive from within living memory of it, and they do not agree -- we keep all four; disagreement is also data. What they share: there was a Tree that held the world coherent. It grew old, and afraid of what its forgetting would unmake, and it summoned mortals to fell it and carry what it carried. Six struck. Six became the Six. The world has been getting by on six-sevenths of itself for a thousand years, and you have grown up inside the missing seventh the way everyone does: hearing it, in the small hours, and being told it's the wind.

  PLAYER 1: Why would the Tree ask to be felled?
    Continue at D308 — Why Machines.
  PLAYER 2: Can it be undone?
    Continue at D310 — Can Be Undone.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D308 · Why Machines
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
The account we hold closest says: fear. It had held everything, remembered everything, for longer than there are numbers -- and it felt its own memory beginning to slip. A god-tree going senile is not a smaller disaster than a god-tree felled; it judged the felling kinder. Whether it was right is not a scribe's question. Whether it changed its mind at the end -- the oldest account has three words scraped out at exactly that place in the telling, and I think about that scraping more than I should.

  PLAYER 1: Did anyone refuse the Tree?
    Continue at D309 — Any Survive.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D309 · Any Survive
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
You have heard something, then. Travelers always have. The Reading holds that a seventh was summoned and did not strike -- and there the agreement ends. One account says the seventh spoke; the accounts of WHAT disagree, and the gods themselves, asked, answer differently, which should frighten you more than any monster in these caves does. The name attached is Naro. The blame attached is total. The evidence attached is... entered, traveler. Beside its variants. Where it belongs. Ask at Quillhold if you want to lose a season; scribes have lost lifetimes to that page, and none of them looked sorry.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D310 · Can Be Undone
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Undone, no. The Tree is down; the Six are what they are; there is no account of a felling running backward. But the slippage -- the failing -- the old pages hold two remedies and rumor of a third. Seal the Root and hold the world still. Let the Choir gather everything into itself. Or the third, the quarrelsome page: renew. A new binding. The margins fight about it. The margins have been fighting about it for six hundred years, which tells you the page matters; dead questions go quiet.

  PLAYER 1: Is the third way possible?
    Continue at D311 — Another Way.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D311 · Another Way
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
The honest entry is: the record does not say. What the record does hold is a pattern -- every few generations, someone sets out to answer that page, and the accounts of them end mid-sentence, and the next hand copies the unfinished entry forward anyway. We are a strange order. We keep even the failures warm, in case one of them turns out to have been a beginning.

D312 · Someone Like You

I take testimony; I don't write prophecy. But you are here, and you are asking, and you have let me talk longer than politeness requires, which the intake manual lists -- truly, page nine -- as a favorable sign in a witness. Walk carefully, traveler. And whatever you end up doing to the world: find a scribe, after. Any scribe. We will want it verbatim.

D313 · About Choir
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
The Choir. Yes. We feel them the way you feel a neighbor through a shared wall -- always there, mostly quiet, occasionally eating a room. They do not mean harm. A river does not mean to erode the bank. The bank, for the record -- and everything I say is for the record -- erodes nonetheless.

  PLAYER 1: They speak well of you, in their way.
