D314 · Speak Well
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
They would. They are generous, in their fashion -- the truce has held for eight hundred years mostly on their patience and our paper. We respect their honesty. We simply wish they would grow more slowly, and they wish we would stop writing down exactly how fast they grow. The correspondence on this point fills nine boxes.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D315 · Hoarding
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
Is a library hoarding books? Don't answer; the Choir has, at length. We keep because without keeping, everything that was becomes compost. Their compost is fertile -- I have seen the groves; they are beautiful; I entered that too, because the record is honest or it is nothing. But the flowers that grow from it do not remember the garden they replaced. Someone has to. We volunteered, a thousand years ago, and we have not yet found a good stopping place.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D274 — Start.

D316 · Opening / return
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
Ah, a new face! How delightful. The Saccharine Concord extends its warmest greetings. We are merchants of the possible, traders in what-remains. How may we sweeten your day?

  PLAYER 1: Who are the Saccharine Concord?

D317 · About Concord
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
We are pragmatists, friend. When the world dissolved -- and it did dissolve, make no mistake -- some sat in the ruins and wept. Others began cataloguing the rubble. We? We started trading it. There is value in everything, even catastrophe. Especially catastrophe.

  PLAYER 1: That's a cynical way to look at the end of the world.
    Continue at D318 — Cynical.
  PLAYER 2: Pragmatism has its virtues.
    Continue at D321 — Pragmatic.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D316 — Start.

D318 · Cynical
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
Cynical? No, no. Realistic. The Rot Choir wants to eat everything. The Recension wants to remember everything. The Pale Curation wants to file everything. The Remnant wants to be left alone. And the world keeps turning regardless. Someone has to keep the economy moving while the philosophers argue about meaning.

  PLAYER 1: Fair point.
    Continue at D316 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Don't you believe in anything beyond commerce?
    Continue at D319 — Beyond Commerce.

D319 · Beyond Commerce
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
Of course we do. We believe in connections. Every trade is a relationship. Every exchange builds trust between strangers. The old world dissolved because people stopped talking to each other and started talking past each other. The Concord keeps the conversations going. Commerce is just the language we use.

  PLAYER 1: That's surprisingly philosophical for a merchant.
    Continue at D320 — Surprisingly Deep.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D316 — Start.

D320 · Surprisingly Deep
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
We take that as a compliment. The Concord learned long ago that the best deals are the ones where both parties feel they've gained something. Sweetness, you see -- it's not deception. It's lubrication. The world moves more smoothly when people are pleasant to each other.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D316 — Start.

D321 · Pragmatic
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
A kindred spirit! The Concord could use more practical minds. The world is full of grand philosophies and empty stomachs. We fill the stomachs. The philosophies can wait.

D322 · What Offer
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
Information, primarily. The Concord has trade routes spanning the surface and the upper caves. We hear things. We know which paths are safe, which ruins have been picked clean, and which factions are feeling friendly this week. Knowledge is the most valuable commodity.

  PLAYER 1: Any tips for the caves below?

D323 · Cave Tips
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
Avoid the spore-thick corridors unless you want to breathe Choir. The temporal anomalies on the lower levels are disorienting but rarely lethal -- just don't trust your sense of direction near them. And if you find old-world tech, bring it to us before the Pale Curation gets their archival gloves on it. We pay better.

  PLAYER 1: Good to know. Thanks.
    Continue at D316 — Start.

D324 · What Trade
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
Everything and anything. Salvage from the old world, fresh provisions, weapons, information, introductions. We are the connective tissue of the post-Felling economy. If it exists and someone wants it, we can probably find it.

  PLAYER 1: Where do you get your stock?

D325 · Stock
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
Everywhere. Surface settlements, cave delvers, even the factions themselves. The Choir sometimes trades biomatter -- useful for medicines, if you can stomach the source. The Pale Curation trades duplicates from their archives. The Remnant trades in raw materials. Only the Recension refuses to deal with us -- in testimony, anyway; they buy paper by the bale. They say memory cannot be commodified. We respectfully disagree, and we print their ledgers.

D326 · Trade Choir
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
Carefully. Very carefully. They are not hostile, but their idea of a fair exchange is... different. They once offered us 'a hundred years of composted memory' in exchange for a cart of iron. We accepted. The memories turned out to be... useful, if unsettling. We learned the recipe for a steel alloy that hasn't been forged in centuries.

D327 · Dissolution
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
The Falling-Due. That's our word for it, because that's what happened: the Tree that held the world's accounts came down, and every debt in creation came due the same afternoon. Boundaries stopped holding. Names stopped sticking. Some things resolidified. Some didn't. We are the ones who learned to trade in the meantime -- and the meantime, friend, has lasted a thousand years.

  PLAYER 1: Others call it different things.
    Continue at D328 — Different Names.
  PLAYER 2: What caused it?
    Continue at D330 — What Caused.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.

D328 · Different Names
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
The Choir barely names it -- ask them and they will tell you what the falling fed. The Recension keeps four accounts of it and refuses to pick one, which is very them. The Pale Curation files it: one event, one folder, a thousand years of appendices. The Remnant says nothing at all. Names reveal what you lost. We lost the ability to pretend anything is permanent. So: the Falling-Due. Everything owed, all at once.

  PLAYER 1: Which name is most accurate?
    Continue at D329 — Most Accurate.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D316 — Start.

D329 · Most Accurate
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
All of them. That's the uncomfortable truth. The world dissolved, burned, severed, fed, and unraveled all at once. Each faction saw the part of the catastrophe that mattered most to them. The Concord sees all the parts, which is why we don't take sides. Bad for business.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D316 — Start.

D330 · What Caused
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
The Tree asked for it. That's the strangest part, and we have had a thousand years to get used to it. It was old, and it had begun to forget -- and when the thing that holds the world forgets, the world goes with it. So it commissioned its own felling, you could say, and paid six mortals in godhood. There was meant to be a seventh; ask a scribe, they love that part. What matters for trade: the world has run short one function ever since, and scarcity, friend, is the beginning of every market.

  PLAYER 1: Can it be fixed?
    Continue at D331 — Can Fix.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D316 — Start.

D331 · Can Fix
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
The Recension thinks so. The Choir hopes not. The Concord? We think it's the wrong question. You don't fix a river -- you build bridges across it. The world changed. We adapt. Adaptation is more profitable than restoration, and considerably less risky.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D316 — Start.

Ah, the grand tapestry of post-Felling society. Where shall we begin? We know everyone, and everyone owes us favors.

D333 · Faction Choir

The big mushroom collective. Impressive scope, questionable dining habits. They want to eat everything and become everything. Good trading partners, actually -- they have no concept of personal ownership, so they'll trade anything they have. Just don't accept dinner invitations.

D334 · Faction Palimpsest
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
The scribes. Very smart, very sad, very difficult to pin down for a business meeting -- there is always one more account to enter first. Their founder took Memory at the Felling and hasn't had a body in seven hundred years; she reads through the lot of them, they say, which makes negotiations interesting. They won't sell testimony on principle. We think that's rich, coming from people who charge a copying fee.

D335 · Faction Curation
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
The archivists. Cold, thorough, absolutely insufferable at parties. They preserve everything -- and I mean the bodies, friend; filed under their last words; you get used to it. Their vaults are the most complete keeping of what was lost. Their founder salt-cured herself alive six hundred years ago and her file still says 'continuing,' and nobody in that faction finds the file strange. Good customers, though. They always pay on time, and they buy salt by the caravan.

  PLAYER 1: Tell me about another faction.

D336 · Faction Remnant
SACCHARINE ENVOY:
The quiet ones. Survivors of something they call the Burning -- and that word is the whole of what anyone knows; no account corroborates them, no god claims them, and they have never once corrected a guess. We've learned not to ask. They're tough, practical, and deeply private. Good fighters, poor conversationalists. They trade in raw materials -- glass, metal, stone. Nothing sentimental. Nothing that requires explanation.

  PLAYER 1: Tell me about another faction.
    Continue at D332 — Other Factions.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D316 — Start.

Pale curator

D338 · About Curation
PALE CURATOR:
We are preservationists. Before the Felling there was no need of us; the Tree forgot nothing. After it, everything began to be lost, and someone had to hold what remained. We hold it. The Recension believes memory lives in the telling. We know it lives in the kept body, indexed and continuing. Not mysticism. Method.

  PLAYER 1: How are you different from the Recension?
    Continue at D339 — Diff Palimpsest.
  PLAYER 2: What's the point of cataloguing a broken world?
    Continue at D342 — Whats The Point.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D337 — Start.

D339 · Diff Palimpsest
PALE CURATOR:
The Recension takes testimony and weeps over it -- they read the dead aloud; they enter their own griefs beside the record. We study. They are the wound's diary. We are the medical record. Both have value, but only one can be consulted without the risk of emotional contamination. Our files are objective. Their Reading is not. They say the same of us, with the adjectives reversed.

  PLAYER 1: Objectivity has its limits.
    Continue at D340 — Objectivity Limits.
  PLAYER 2: I see the value in your approach.
    Continue at D341 — See Value.
  PLAYER 3: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D337 — Start.

D340 · Objectivity Limits
PALE CURATOR:
Noted. Filed under: dissenting opinions, subcategory: philosophical. We do not claim perfection. We claim thoroughness. The Curation's archives have saved more knowledge than every other faction combined. Facts outlast feelings.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D337 — Start.

D341 · See Value
PALE CURATOR:
Acknowledged. The Curation appreciates rational minds. If you encounter pre-Felling artifacts in your travels, the Curation would be interested in examining them. We catalog, we do not confiscate. Items are returned after documentation.

  PLAYER 1: I'll keep that in mind.
    Continue at D337 — Start.

D342 · Whats The Point
PALE CURATOR:
The point is continuity. The Felling cut the world in two -- before and after. Without records, the 'before' ceases to exist. Without understanding the 'before,' the 'after' has no context. Without context, every mistake the old world made can be repeated. The Curation exists to prevent repetition.

  PLAYER 1: What mistakes are you trying to prevent?
    Continue at D343 — What Mistakes.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D337 — Start.

D343 · What Mistakes
PALE CURATOR:
The primary one. The Rot Choir consumes what should be kept. Every body the substrate takes is a record lost to the file, and the file is the only continuity this world has left. And beneath that error runs the older one: the world's holding is finite, and failing, and everyone who might mend it is instead arguing about what to call it. The consequences would be catastrophic. We have projections. We do not enjoy them.

  PLAYER 1: So you're against the Rot Choir?
    Continue at D344 — Against Choir.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D337 — Start.

D344 · Against Choir
PALE CURATOR:
We are against ignorance. The Choir is not malicious -- they simply do not understand the long-term impact of their growth. Or they do not care. The distinction is irrelevant from an archival perspective. The damage is the same.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D337 — Start.

D345 · Severance
PALE CURATOR:
The Felling. Date: year zero; all reckoning proceeds from it. Event: the felling of the world-binding Tree by six of seven summoned; ascension of the six; the seventh inheritance unclaimed. Casualties: unquantifiable. Status: ongoing.

  PLAYER 1: Ongoing? It's still happening?

D346 · Still Happening
PALE CURATOR:
The holding weakens. The failure the deep villages call the Thinning proceeds at a measurable rate; manifestation events have doubled within two generations. By the Curation's projections, the Root's holding-field reaches critical levels within two to five generations. We do not publish this. You asked.

  PLAYER 1: What happens at critical levels?
    Continue at D347 — Critical Levels.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.

D347 · Critical Levels
PALE CURATOR:
Unknown. Our models extrapolate from limited data. Best case: localized failures of name and place. Worst case: general unbinding. The distinction between a thing and the next thing ceases to hold. Everything becomes... undifferentiated. We would prefer to avoid this outcome.

  PLAYER 1: How do we prevent it?
    Continue at D348 — Prevent.
  PLAYER 2: Back to my other questions.

D348 · Prevent
PALE CURATOR:
The Curation recommends sealing the Root. Held in stone, tended in perpetuity. The world would stop losing -- and stop healing; we do not pretend otherwise. Our holdings are physical. A still world suits the file. We are aware of how that sounds.

  PLAYER 1: That's convenient for you.
    Continue at D349 — Convenient.

D349 · Convenient
PALE CURATOR:
The Curation does not set policy based on self-interest. We set policy based on data. The data indicates that the Thinning is an existential threat. The solution with the highest probability of success is the seal. We are sorry for the collateral effects, but the alternative is worse.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.
    Continue at D337 — Start.

D350 · Other Option
PALE CURATOR:
The Recension keeps a page they call the Three Dispositions; the third speaks of renewal -- a new binding, a new Tree. The Curation has insufficient data to evaluate its feasibility. It may be possible. It may also be wishful thinking from an order that prefers hope shelved where it can reach it. We recommend caution with unverified hypotheses.

  PLAYER 1: I'll investigate further.
    Continue at D337 — Start.

D351 · Reading File
PALE CURATOR:
We are always reading from a file. The file is the world. Every observation is recorded, cross-referenced, and made available for future consultation. If our manner seems clinical, it is because clinical precision saves lives. Imprecision killed the old world.

  PLAYER 1: Fair enough.
    Continue at D337 — Start.

D352 · Archives
PALE CURATOR:
Everything recoverable from the pre-Felling world. Technical schematics, cultural records, biological specimens, geological surveys, personal effects. The Curation does not discriminate between significant and trivial. All data has potential value. A shopping list from the old world tells us about their economy. A child's drawing tells us about their cognition.

  PLAYER 1: Have you found anything about how the Felling was performed?

D353 · Substrate Machines
PALE CURATOR:
Fragments. Accounts of the rite itself -- the marked place, the passing of the axe, the order of the strikes. Enough to understand what was done; insufficient to reproduce it. We are aware that certain parties would value the missing portions very highly. That is one of the reasons the file is sealed. There are others.

  PLAYER 1: Could the rite be performed again?

D354 · Machines Exist
PALE CURATOR:
The Curation declines to speculate. What we will say: the Recension sealed certain archives long ago, and unremembered where -- their founder's own doing, and the one act of deliberate forgetting in that order's history. Whatever the rite's missing pages may be, they are beyond everyone's reach. Including ours. The file ends there. Files that end there stay sealed.

  PLAYER 1: Why not?

D355 · Why Not Publish
PALE CURATOR:
Because knowledge without context is dangerous. The old world proved that. When we are satisfied that disclosure will not lead to harm, we will share our findings. Until then, the locations remain classified.

D377 · What Happened Open
GLASSBLOWN DRIFTER:

D378 · Why Glass
GLASSBLOWN DRIFTER:
When you heat sand hot enough, it becomes glass. When you heat a city hot enough... same thing. Our home is a field of glass now. Smooth, clear, beautiful if you don't think about what it used to be. Some of us go back sometimes. Stand on the glass and listen. You can hear echoes underneath. The scribes say those are the under-writing showing through. We just call them ghosts.

D379 · Go Back
GLASSBLOWN DRIFTER:

D380 · Advice

D381 · Dont Trust

D391 · Supplies

A healthy traveller can carry it for later.'

D415 · Annex

D443 · Joining

Slowly, as all true things change. The elders said: the wise do not push the river — they listen for where it wants to flow. The Choir is the river beneath the river. I stopped resisting, and I was already part of it.

  PLAYER 1: Back to my other questions.

D444 · Before
SOLM:
I tended a small grove. Names of plants my grandmother named, in a tongue we no longer speak. Now the names live in the spores. Nothing of value is ever lost — only translated. The grove is wider now. It reaches places my hands could not.

  PLAYER 1: That is a strange comfort.
    Continue at D445 — Comfort.
  PLAYER 2: Back.
    Continue at D442 — Start.

D445 · Comfort

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D442 — Start.

D446 · Teaching
SOLM:
It teaches what the old books taught, but with a longer breath. Balance. Patience. That endings are not endings, only different shapes for the same water. I was not a quick student in life. The Choir is patient with me still.

  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/RotChoir.json · Grib_1]

D451 · Opening / return
VURN:

D452 · Bleak
VURN:
Bleak? No. Bleak is for those who pretend the night will not come. I am only honest. Honesty is a cold thing, but cold things keep. Sentimental things rot — and not even in a useful way, like we do.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D451 — Start.

D453 · Surrender
VURN:
Let. What a small word. The Choir does not take. The Choir is what was already happening, only faster. I had a name. A sister. A small fire by a window. The fire went out. The window broke. The sister was gone before the rest. I was already half-spore by then. So. I joined the rest of myself.

  PLAYER 1: I'm sorry, Vurn.
    Continue at D454 — Sorry.
  PLAYER 2: Back.
    Continue at D451 — Start.

D454 · Sorry
VURN:
Don't be. Pity is wasted on the dying — and we are all dying, just at different speeds. Yours is faster than mine now, ironically. But we are not ungentle. Walk safely, friend. Or do not. Either way, we will see you again.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D451 — Start.

D455 · Hate
VURN:
Hate? No. The living are only us, before. I look at you and see myself, with the lights still on inside. It is hard to hate a candle for not knowing it is short. Easier to feel sorry. But that is also tiresome, so mostly I just watch.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D451 — Start.

D456 · Bloom Ask
VURN:
Early, yes. It has learned your name but not your sentences. Be still a moment. We will speak over you.

  PLAYER 1: Take it back.

  PLAYER 2: Not yet.
    Continue at D451 — Start.

D457 · Bloom Taken
VURN:
There. The ground releases its claim — for now, and without grudge.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D451 — Start.

D458 · Encasement Offer

That is a lot of blood to carry uphill. The wall holds some of us, you know. Warm. Unhurried. It could hold you too, when you are ready to put the weight down.

  PLAYER 1: Yes. Hold me.

D459 · Encasement Declined

D461 · Welcoming
AMAI:
Why not? Strangers and family share the same blood once you trace it back far enough. The mycelium taught me what my grandmother taught me first: nothing is alone. The river is one river, even when it splits.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D460 — Start.

D462 · People
AMAI:
We were many, by the river. We sang to the marsh-rye, and the rye gave its heart back. When the great cold came, the river grew thin. We followed it underground. The Choir was already there, waiting, like an old aunt who does not need to ask why you came home.

  PLAYER 1: That sounds peaceful.
    Continue at D463 — Peace.
  PLAYER 2: Back.
    Continue at D460 — Start.

D463 · Peace
AMAI:
Peace is the wrong word. The river was never peaceful. It worked, always. So do we. But there is rest in working alongside others. That, yes — that part is good. We carry less when we carry together.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D460 — Start.

D464 · Inside
AMAI:
Like a kitchen with many hands and one pot. You hear everyone, even when no one speaks. You do not feel small in it — you feel held. Maybe that is hard for a walker to understand. Walkers are taught to be one. We were taught to be many, even before the spores.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D460 — Start.

D465 · Bloom Ask

You carry a beginning. Beginnings can be returned. Kneel, if kneeling is easy for you — the soil will not keep what we ask it to release.

D466 · Bloom Taken

D479 · Sagas
KETCH:
We carry our shapes from before. The tellings were how my people held the ones the soil had taken back — in the telling itself, you understand. The Choir holds its own the same way. Same craft, deeper roots. The six tired gods would not blink at us.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D478 — Start.

D480 · Fate
KETCH:
Fate. Hah. It is the cloth that is woven whether you watch or not. The Choir is the loom now. It does not care if the threads sing — only that they hold. Yet some of us still sing. That is our small rebellion. Or perhaps it is part of the weave too. Who knows.

  PLAYER 1: Strong words.
    Continue at D481 — Strong.
  PLAYER 2: Back.
    Continue at D478 — Start.

D481 · Strong
KETCH:
Strong words for a soft creature, yes. We were never as fierce as we pretended. But we did not need to be — only honest about the cold, and willing to row through it. Walk steady, walker. The cold finds us all.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D478 — Start.

D482 · Sea
KETCH:
The sea, yes. Always. But the sea was never just water — it was the long song between storms. The Choir has its own long song. Different key. Same listening. I do not miss what I still have, only renamed.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D478 — Start.

D488 · Who

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D487 — Start.

D489 · Self
AN ENCASED ELDER:
Less than you would like and more than you fear. I remember the shape of a doorway I have not stood in for four hundred years. I do not remember minding. You are asking whether something was lost. Something was moved.

  PLAYER 1: That isn't the same as nothing being lost.
    Continue at D490 — Not Same.
  PLAYER 2: Back.
    Continue at D487 — Start.

D490 · Not Same
AN ENCASED ELDER:
You are counting on a ledger we stopped keeping. Nothing left. It went on, in a body with more of it. Say lost if the word helps you stand here — walkers need the word, and we are patient about the need. But do not put it in our mouth. Everything here is somebody. That is not comfort. That is the inventory. You are permitted to think otherwise, and still drink at our seep.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D487 — Start.

D491 · Node
AN ENCASED ELDER:
The gather-point. Every thread in this vault runs to it, and from it to the next vault, and the next — under the whole country, if you had the patience to follow one. We travel that way. It is not walking and it is not pleasant and it is very fast.

  PLAYER 1: Could I travel that way?
    Continue at D492 — Travel.
  PLAYER 2: Back.
    Continue at D487 — Start.

D492 · Travel
AN ENCASED ELDER:
You could be carried that way. Not the same verb. The substrate decides where a carried thing is set down, and the substrate is not consulting you about it. Ask it of the grove, not of me — I am one node of it and I do not have the whole map, and neither, I think, does the grove.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D487 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

Local story: Cinderhold — The Drowned Level

R001 · Elder
  [offer]
  [Offer only after all predecessor concerns are complete and Requires predicates pass; failure may show Withheld.]
You'll have heard the pump, or you'll have heard it stop, which is worse. The lower level's under water to the knee and climbing. My grandmother's lot dug that level with their hands, and her lot said the water came up because the grove wanted its spring back. That's an old story; it's a cracked pump. The rod's sheared and the binding's rotted through. Iron spikes and an oak haft would see it right, and a leather binding, and the smith sells all three if you'll carry them. Some of the lads went down to bail and came up fast with something behind them, out in the field north of town. And if you've no hands for iron, fire clay will plug the seep. It'll stop the water. I'm told it stops the spring as well.

  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Data/Concerns/Cinderhold.json:10]

Local story: the First Tent — The Clan at the Poles

R021 · TentRightHost
  [offer]
  [Offer only after all predecessor concerns are complete and Requires predicates pass; failure may show Withheld.]
There is water, and there is shade; sit, and let the hard part wait until you have drunk. My guest is a salt-carrier. She took my cloth four days ago with my own clan behind her, and they are behind her still: they are sitting at the poles. She broke a carriage-contract with them and walked off the pans with the salt. I have not asked her business. They told me anyway. They keep the oath to the hour, as they should; at dawn her third day is over, and they will take her. And then I stand between my guest and my own blood, which is what I swore here, under these poles, and what I would rather not find out I meant. Hear me plainly: I am not asking you to fight my clan. They are people, and the oath binds you as it binds them. There are other ways out of the morning.

People chalk their names there so the east knows who they are, and somebody ought to read them out loud, every name. I won't. The lamp-man won't. ========================================================================
08 · WHAT STILLLEAF KEPT
========================================================================
  [Story chain]
The shipped middle-game chain: Hollin in Quillhold gives the keeper’s last words; Teodra Halm at the Salt-Vault indexes the file and negotiates the key; the player descends through Stillleaf to the sealed register and keeper’s slate; then chooses custody and reports the outcome.


1. Quillhold — Hollin’s search
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/StillleafArchive.json · StillleafSearcher_1]
The Searcher explains the need and supplies the keeper’s last words. Accepting and releasing are alternative actions.


D493 · Opening / return
HOLLIN VESK:
Hollin Vesk does not look up from a survey map until she has finished the line she is tracing. 'You are not Recension. Good. I am a Searcher, which the Mainline tolerates the way it tolerates weather. Ask, or don't.'

  PLAYER 1: What does a Searcher search for?
    Continue at D494 — Library.
  PLAYER 2: You said you had work for an outsider.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits accept.
    Continue at D495 — Contract.
  PLAYER 3: Here is the Stillleaf register.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits deliver.
    Continue at D503 — Delivered.
  PLAYER 4: About Stillleaf: I must tell you what I did.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits report.
    Continue at D505 — Reported.
  PLAYER 5: I am done searching. Release me from it.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits release.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 6: The Three Dispositions name three stones that would hold the world still. Have you memory-marble for the sealing?
    Available when: when Keep Memory Marble Given has not been recorded; before any ending.
    Continue at D623 — Stone.
  PLAYER 7: [Release] I won't be sealing the Root. I wanted to tell you myself.
    Available when: while Keep the World is active.
    Continue at D624 — Keep Released.
  PLAYER 8: What has changed?
    Available when: after Renewal through a vessel.
    Continue at D629 — After1.
  PLAYER 9: What has changed?
    Available when: after Renewal through practice.
    Continue at D633 — After2.
  PLAYER 10: What has changed?
    Available when: after Gathered.
    Continue at D637 — After3.
  PLAYER 11: What has changed?
    Available when: after Kept.
    Continue at D641 — After4.
  PLAYER 12: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D494 · Library
HOLLIN VESK:
'The Sealed Libraries. The Reader un-remembered them so that no one could make the Recension open them, and the Recension keeps that bargain. I keep catalogue fragments instead: three shelf-marks from a place called Stillleaf, copied by a hand that is dead. The Mainline cannot look for it without breaking faith. An outsider can, at no cost to anyone but the outsider.'

  PLAYER 1: Then tell me the work.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits accept.
    Continue at D495 — Contract.
  PLAYER 2: Back.
    Continue at D493 — Start.
  PLAYER 3: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D495 · Contract
HOLLIN VESK:
'Stillleaf's last keeper was preserved at the Salt-Vault, six chunks south and one east of here. Pale Curation files its dead by their last words, and I have the keeper's. Say them to an Indexer and the file opens; the keeper's key should be filed beside the body. Curation will ask something for it. Hear them out; I make no promises on their behalf. Bring the register to light. That is the whole of what I ask, and I will pay you in standing with people who keep grudges for centuries.'

  PLAYER 1: I will carry the words.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits accept.
    Continue at D496 — Accepted.
  PLAYER 2: Not now.
    Continue at D493 — Start.
  PLAYER 3: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D496 · Accepted
HOLLIN VESK:
She writes the words on a slip and hands it over. 'Keeping is not the same as showing. Those are the keeper's, not mine. South and east to the Salt-Vault; the Indexers are the ones with the ledgers. When you have the register, I am here.'

  PLAYER 1: Understood.
    Conversation ends.

R423 · Hollin Vesk
  [Stillleaf archive / TryConversation]
  [Accept the Stillleaf search; keeper last words are taught and directions recorded in quest journal.]
Hollin: 'The keeper of Stillleaf was preserved at the Salt-Vault, six chunks south and one east of here. Pale Curation files its dead by their last words, and the keeper's were these: "Keeping is not the same as showing." Say them to an Indexer and the file opens; the key should be filed beside the keeper. Whatever Curation asks for it, hear them out. I want the register found. I have not said what should happen to it after.' [Q] keeps these directions.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/StillleafArchiveContent.cs:146]

R424 · Hollin Vesk
  [Stillleaf archive / TryConversation]
  [Decline the Stillleaf search; clean refusal.]
Hollin: 'Then it stays lost a while longer. That is not a debt; the Searchers have waited longer than you have been alive. What I told you is yours to keep.'

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/StillleafArchiveContent.cs:151]

2. The Salt-Vault — the keeper’s file and key
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/StillleafArchive.json · StillleafIndexer_1]
Recite the last words, then agree, bargain or refuse. Agree/bargain release the file; refusal leaves the key with the keeper.


D497 · Opening / return
TEODRA HALM:
Teodra Halm does not look up. 'Intake or retrieval? The Salt-Vault does not receive social calls, and I am not the window for either.' A gloved hand rests on a ledger whose every line ends the same way.

  PLAYER 1: I am looking for a keeper's file.
    Continue at D498 — Index.
  PLAYER 2: I have the keeper's last words. Keeping is not the same as showing.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits retrieve.
    Continue at D499 — File.
  PLAYER 3: About the request you made for the key.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits agree.
    Continue at D500 — Request.
  PLAYER 4: Here is the Stillleaf register, to be filed.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits file.
    Continue at D504 — Filed.
  PLAYER 5: About Stillleaf: I must tell you what I did.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits report.
    Continue at D506 — Noted.
  PLAYER 6: Curation keeps mute-stone. I need it for the sealing of the Root.
    Available when: when Keep Mute Stone Given has not been recorded; before any ending.
    Continue at D625 — Mute Stone.
  PLAYER 7: [Release] I won't be sealing the Root. I wanted to tell you myself.
    Available when: while Keep the World is active.
    Continue at D626 — Keep Released.
  PLAYER 8: What has changed?
    Available when: after Renewal through a vessel.
    Continue at D630 — After1.
  PLAYER 9: What has changed?
    Available when: after Renewal through practice.
    Continue at D634 — After2.
  PLAYER 10: What has changed?
    Available when: after Gathered.
    Continue at D638 — After3.
  PLAYER 11: What has changed?
    Available when: after Kept.
    Continue at D642 — After4.
  PLAYER 12: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D498 · Index
TEODRA HALM:
'Then you have nothing. We file the preserved by their last words: the final sentence, entered exactly, argued over for a week if need be. A name is not an index. A place is not an index. Bring me the words and I will find the file; bring me anything else and I will file you under: visitors, unproductive.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D497 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D499 · File
TEODRA HALM:
The gloved hand closes the ledger on the entry. 'Keeper, Stillleaf; salt-cured; effects: one iron key, filed beside. Status: continuing. The key is Curation's to release, and the Curation has a request. Not a toll. A request.'

  PLAYER 1: Name it.
    Continue at D500 — Request.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D500 · Request
TEODRA HALM:
'If you open Stillleaf, bring what you find here, to be filed, not read. The keeper is a vessel we hold; the register is paper, and paper is lossy, and it belongs beside the body it describes. Agree, and the file is released. Refuse, and it stays with its keeper, which is also correct.'

  PLAYER 1: Agreed. I will bring it here to be filed.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits agree.
    Continue at D501 — Released.
  PLAYER 2: I will bring it here. I do not promise it stays.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits bargain.
    Continue at D501 — Released.
  PLAYER 3: No. I will not promise that.
    Available when: Stillleaf Archive permits refuse.
    Continue at D502 — Refused.
  PLAYER 4: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D501 · Released
TEODRA HALM:
'Entered.' The Indexer unlocks the salt file beside the desk without rising and returns to the ledger. 'Take the key yourself. I do not hand things; I file them.'

  PLAYER 1: Understood.
    Conversation ends.

D502 · Refused
TEODRA HALM:
'A clean no files clean. The key stays with its keeper; the words will still open the file if you return.'

  PLAYER 1: Understood.
    Conversation ends.

The register and the keeper’s slate
  [Examine the register after entering Stillleaf’s sealed archive.]
READING / EXAMINATION:
A bound register in pale covers, the name Stillleaf pressed into its spine. It lists what this archive held — shelf, hand and year for every entry — and nothing of what the entries said. A slate is tied to the cover in the keeper's hand: 'Whoever carries this out: I sealed this place so that it would stay sealed. Not because what is here is dangerous. Because it was given to me to keep, and keeping is not the same as showing. If you take it, choose who holds it after you as carefully as I chose no one.'

  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Blueprints/Objects.json#StillleafRegister]

3A. Custody alternative — deliver the register to Quillhold
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/StillleafArchive.json · StillleafSearcher_1]
After the Stillleaf descent and the keeper’s slate: this is one possible custody outcome. It excludes 3B and 3C.

4. Tell Hollin what happened
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/StillleafArchive.json · StillleafSearcher_1]

========================================================================
09 · THE TWO HOUSES
========================================================================
  [Optional household drama]
House Vex and House Thresker have separate branching family dramas. Read each household’s introductions and evidence before confrontation and resolution; their outcomes are independent of the four world endings.


Drama Vex Diminished Head
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/HouseVex.json · Drama_Vex_DiminishedHead]

D507 · Opening / return
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
Theron Vex stands among racked essences, turning a sealed vial against the light. The distillery air is bitter with old work. 'Traveler. Mind the shelves — everything in here is labeled twice, and we keep it that way.'

  PLAYER 1: What does House Vex make here?
    Continue at D508 — Craft Explain.
  PLAYER 2: You carry yourself like a man waiting for bad news.
    Continue at D515 — Theron Weight.
  PLAYER 3: Tell me about Lyra.
    Available when: Drama Active: Vex.
    Continue at D517 — Lyra Grief.
  PLAYER 4: I have proof of where Kess's bottles end up.
    Available when: Drama Active: Vex; Witness Knows: Vex:theron:poisons_reach_warlords.
    Continue at D519 — Warlord Truth.
  PLAYER 5: It's time to choose, Theron. Kess, or Vorus's mission.
    Available when: Drama Active: Vex; Witness Knows: Vex:theron:poisons_reach_warlords; Path Taken: Vex:FounderLetter:LetterUncovered; Pressure Point State: Vex:PoisonMerchant:active.
    Continue at D521 — Final Choice.
  PLAYER 6: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D508 · Craft Explain
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'Essences. Antivenins, febrifuges, dyes that hold their color for a lifetime. My father Vorus pulled medicine out of the most murderous things in this jungle.' He sets the vial down with exact care. 'Lately we bottle other orders.'

  PLAYER 1: Tell me about Vorus.
    Continue at D509 — Vorus Legacy.
  PLAYER 2: What other orders?
    Continue at D512 — Kess Demand.
  PLAYER 3: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D509 · Vorus Legacy
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'He believed venom was medicine that hadn't been asked politely. Eighty-seven years old and he'd still walk the canopy paths for a green mamba. He thought the jungle would guard its own secrets from bad men.' A tired breath. 'The jungle did not.'

  PLAYER 1: He wrote his intent down somewhere, didn't he?
    Available when: Drama Active: Vex; Witness Knows: Vex:theron:poisons_reach_warlords; Pressure Point State: Vex:FounderLetter:active.
    Continue at D510 — Ask Letter.
  PLAYER 2: A better creed than most.
    Continue at D507 — Start.
  PLAYER 3: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D510 · Ask Letter
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
Theron studies you for a long moment — the way a distiller reads a label twice. 'There is a letter. Locked in my study. I've shown it to no one since Lyra died.' He weighs a small key in his palm. 'You brought me the truth about Kess. I'll trade you Vorus's.'

  PLAYER 1: Read it with me.
    Continue at D511 — Letter Shared.
After this choice: Vorus's letter is plain: every essence of House Vex was meant to heal. It is dated the year the distillery was raised.


D511 · Letter Shared
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
Theron reads it aloud, slowly, and his voice does not falter until the last line. ''To distill is to decide what a dangerous thing becomes.'' He folds the letter with both hands. 'Nyla should hear this. All of it.'

  PLAYER 1: She will.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: What will you do now?
    Continue at D520 — Theron Resolve.

D512 · Kess Demand
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'Kess Ballow wants the entire monsoon harvest, early. Every gland, every fang, every specimen we can strip out of the wet season. If I give him that, the jungle gives us nothing next year.' His jaw sets. 'If I refuse, I find out what his patience is worth.'

  PLAYER 1: Refuse him. The jungle isn't his to empty.
    Available when: Drama Active: Vex; Pressure Point State: Vex:VenomVault:active.
    Continue at D513 — Harvest Refused.
After this choice: Theron will harvest with the old restraint and let Kess wait.

  PLAYER 2: Give him the harvest. Buy your family time.
    Available when: Drama Active: Vex; Pressure Point State: Vex:VenomVault:active.
    Continue at D514 — Harvest Stripped.
After this choice: The vats will run full this season. The jungle will remember it.

  PLAYER 3: That's a hard ledger.
    Continue at D507 — Start.

D513 · Harvest Refused
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'Then it's decided. We take what the jungle can spare and not a fang more.' Saying it seems to cost him something and pay him back at once. 'Kess will hear his no by monsoon's end.'

  PLAYER 1: Hold to it.
    Conversation ends.

D514 · Harvest Stripped
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'Full crates, then. I'll tell the harvesters to take the nests whole.' He does not look toward the jungle when he says it. 'Vorus walked those paths for sixty years and never once emptied one.'

  PLAYER 1: It buys you time.
    Conversation ends.

D515 · Theron Weight
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'Waiting is most of what I do now. I meet Kess's orders with one hand and draft my refusal with the other, and I tell myself a man can do both.' He almost smiles. 'You don't have to say it. I know what half-measures distill down to.'

  PLAYER 1: Which hand wins?
    Continue at D516 — Press Theron.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D516 · Press Theron
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'The one holding my family. Whichever that turns out to be.' He rubs his eyes. 'If you want to know what my hesitation costs, ask Sael. She loads the crates. She watches the road they leave on.' It is as close to asking for help as he will come.

  PLAYER 1: I'll speak with her.
    Continue at D507 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D517 · Lyra Grief
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'My granddaughter. Eleven years old. She wanted to learn the craft so badly she let herself into the distillery alone.' The words come level, worn smooth from repetition. 'One bottle was mislabeled. One. We mark everything twice now.'

  PLAYER 1: Does Nyla know how it happened?
    Continue at D518 — Lyra Secret.
  PLAYER 2: I'm sorry.
    Continue at D507 — Start.

D518 · Lyra Secret
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'Nyla knows her cousin died. Not the how of it. I couldn't put that in the house along with the grief.' He looks at the twice-marked shelves. 'Maybe the how is the part that would have kept her out of Kess's arithmetic.'

  PLAYER 1: Maybe she should hear it.
    Continue at D507 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D519 · Warlord Truth
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
You lay it out: the river route, the camps, the warcolors flying over crates Sael sealed with her own hands. Theron listens the way a man listens to a diagnosis he already suspected. 'Murder, then. Not commerce. I have suspected it since the second contract and called my suspicion caution.'

  PLAYER 1: It's certainty now. Use it.
    Continue at D520 — Theron Resolve.
After this choice: Theron's suspicion has hardened into knowledge.


D520 · Theron Resolve
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'Then everything in this house changes, or nothing does.' He sets both hands flat on the workbench. 'Before I choose, I want Vorus's own words in front of me, and I want my daughter's eyes clear when I read them. Bring me those two things and I'll give you a decision.'

  PLAYER 1: You'll have them.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D521 · Final Choice
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
Theron stands among the racked essences with the letter folded inside his coat and no more room left to wait. 'Say it plainly, traveler. You've seen every side of this house. What should House Vex become?'

  PLAYER 1: A healing house. Name Nyla co-leader on Vorus's terms.
    Available when: Path Taken: Vex:TaintedHeir:NylaAwakens.
    Continue at D522 — Resolved Rebuild.
After this choice: House Vex returns to the founder's mission, with Nyla bound to it.

  PLAYER 2: Keep Kess's account. Pour his money into medicine.
    Continue at D523 — Resolved Compromise.
After this choice: House Vex will heal with one hand and arm killers with the other.

  PLAYER 3: Cut Kess off entirely. Whatever it costs.
    Continue at D524 — Resolved Sever.
After this choice: House Vex severs the Ballow contract. The lean seasons begin.

  PLAYER 4: I can't make this choice for you.
    Continue at D507 — Start.

D522 · Resolved Rebuild
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'Then it's done. Nyla takes the bench beside mine, and the first essence we rack under her name will be a cure.' For the first time he looks like a man whose craft still belongs to him. 'Vorus would have liked you.'

  PLAYER 1: Good luck, Theron.
    Conversation ends.

D523 · Resolved Compromise
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'Blood money for bandages.' He says it aloud to find out whether he can live with the taste. Apparently he can. 'Vorus kept this family fed through worse. I'll tell myself that until it's true.'

  PLAYER 1: It's survival.
    Conversation ends.

D524 · Resolved Sever
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
'I'll send the refusal with the next courier. No craft worth keeping is bought with what Kess pays.' He begins clearing Kess's standing order from the board, slowly, like a man taking down a fever chart. 'We'll be poorer by morning. Cleaner, too.'

  PLAYER 1: You chose well.
    Conversation ends.

D525 · End
DRAMA VEX DIMINISHED HEAD:
Theron returns to his vials. The bitter smell of the distillery settles back around him.


Drama Vex Rising Inheritor
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/HouseVex.json · Drama_Vex_RisingInheritor]

D526 · Opening / return
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
Nyla Vex is repricing a crate of stoppered vials, chalking figures onto slate faster than you can read them. 'If you're buying, antivenins are on the left. If you're selling, I set the price. If you're here to talk—' she finishes a sum, '—talk quickly.'

  PLAYER 1: What do the figures say?
    Continue at D527 — Ambition.
  PLAYER 2: What's Kess Ballow to your house?
    Continue at D529 — Kess View.
  PLAYER 3: Do you know how your cousin Lyra died?
    Available when: Drama Active: Vex.
    Continue at D531 — Lyra Truth.
  PLAYER 4: Kess pays best. Take his contracts and don't look back.
    Available when: Drama Active: Vex; Pressure Point State: Vex:TaintedHeir:active.
    Continue at D533 — Sideshift.
  PLAYER 5: You need to see where Kess's bottles actually go.
    Available when: Drama Active: Vex; Pressure Point State: Vex:TaintedHeir:active; Witness Knows: Vex:theron:poisons_reach_warlords; Witness Knows: Vex:theron:competing_merchant_valdris.
    Continue at D535 — Confrontation.
  PLAYER 6: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D527 · Ambition
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
'They say we're the finest venom house in the basin and priced like a village apothecary. My grandfather gave essences away to anyone with a cough.' She taps the slate. 'I intend for House Vex to be worth what it's worth. Call that greed if you like. Greed with clean labels.'

  PLAYER 1: And if the buyers aren't clean?
    Continue at D530 — Kess Doubt.
  PLAYER 2: What's wrong with being a village apothecary?
    Continue at D528 — Ambition Deep.
  PLAYER 3: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D528 · Ambition Deep
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
'Nothing — if you want your life's work to fit inside one village and die there.' Something that isn't quite anger crosses her face. 'I've watched my father shrink every season since Lyra. I won't spend my life measuring doses in a place the maps forget.'

  PLAYER 1: Respect and wealth aren't the same purchase.
    Continue at D526 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D529 · Kess View
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
'Our best customer. He pays triple rate, in advance, in coin that doesn't bounce.' She recites it like a defense she has given before. 'High-value compounds for discreet buyers. That isn't crime, it's commerce. Every trade house in the basin would take his contract.'

  PLAYER 1: Do you know what his buyers do with it?
    Continue at D530 — Kess Doubt.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D530 · Kess Doubt
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
'What buyers do with their purchases is their ledger, not mine.' The chalk stops for half a line. 'Father says the same thing about Kess in the opposite direction, so one of us is lying to ourselves. I've decided it's him.'

  PLAYER 1: You don't sound decided.
    Continue at D526 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D531 · Lyra Truth
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
You tell her what Theron never did: the mislabeled bottle, the symbols an eleven-year-old trusted, how fast it was. Nyla puts the chalk down with exaggerated care. 'I was told it was an accident. A vague one. Nobody ever said the label lied.'

  PLAYER 1: Labels are the whole craft. That's the point.
    Continue at D532 — Lyra After.
After this choice: Nyla knows how Lyra really died now.


D532 · Lyra After
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
'One wrong word on a bottle.' She looks at the crate of vials she has been pricing as if it has just changed species. 'And I've been telling myself that what's written on ours is somebody else's ledger.'

  PLAYER 1: Think on it.
    Conversation ends.

D533 · Sideshift
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
'Finally. Somebody in this village who can count.' Her chin lifts; whatever had been undecided in her settles the profitable way. 'Kess wants a standing contract and Father won't sign it. I'll manage the account myself, and House Vex can thank me when the roof doesn't leak.'

  PLAYER 1: Now you're talking sense.
    Continue at D534 — Sideshift After.
After this choice: Nyla has committed to Kess's coin. Theron has lost his ally.

  PLAYER 2: Wait. Forget I said that.
    Continue at D526 — Start.

D534 · Sideshift After
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
'Tell Kess his next order ships on schedule.' The slate fills with new figures — bigger ones. She does not look up again.

  PLAYER 1: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D535 · Confrontation
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
You spread it in front of her: the river route, the warcolor camps, Vex seals broken open on spear racks. And beside it, Valdris's standing offer — healing compounds, open ledgers, the same margins. She reads all of it twice. 'These numbers are real,' she says finally. Quietly. 'Both sets.'

  PLAYER 1: Kess's coin is murder with good bookkeeping. Valdris's isn't. Choose.
    Continue at D536 — Awakened.
After this choice: Nyla has seen the proof, and the alternative. Something fundamental has shifted.


D536 · Awakened
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
For a long moment she is very still — the stillness of a sum that will not come out any other way. 'I priced those shipments. I chalked margins on dead villages and called it commerce.' She wipes the slate clean with her sleeve. 'Get me my father. House Vex is renegotiating.'

  PLAYER 1: He'll want you beside him.
    Conversation ends.

D537 · End
DRAMA VEX RISING INHERITOR:
Nyla goes back to her figures, chalk clicking against the slate.


Drama Vex Named Antagonist
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/HouseVex.json · Drama_Vex_NamedAntagonist]

D538 · Opening / return
DRAMA VEX NAMED ANTAGONIST:
Kess Ballow keeps his table at the village edge with his back to the buildings and his eyes on the road — never on the treeline. Rings on every finger, ledger squared to the table's corner. 'The traveler. Sit, don't sit. Everyone is somebody's customer eventually.'

  PLAYER 1: What's your business with House Vex?
    Continue at D539 — Business.
  PLAYER 2: You sit a long way from the jungle for a jungle trader.
    Continue at D541 — Jungle Fear.
  PLAYER 3: I know what your buyers do with Vex essences.
    Available when: Drama Active: Vex; Witness Knows: Vex:theron:poisons_reach_warlords.

  PLAYER 4: I have a proposition worth more than your poison trade.
    Available when: Drama Active: Vex; Witness Knows: Vex:theron:competing_merchant_valdris; Pressure Point State: Vex:PoisonMerchant:active.
    Continue at D546 — Valdris Offer.
  PLAYER 5: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D539 · Business
DRAMA VEX NAMED ANTAGONIST:

========================================================================
  [Main-story context]
The catacomb and founding-village voices introduce the wall, the Rooted and the places beneath. These are contextual encounters on an inward journey, not a written audience in which the player obtains a god’s permission.


The Rooted
  [Examine the Rooted in the founding village.]
READING / EXAMINATION:
A human shape has grown into the stone: shins flat, knees bent, torso arched above the floor. Pale fungus rises from his chest. His open arms reach toward the eastern wall and stop short of it. Nothing has been built in that narrow distance. The pose holds no strain.

  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Blueprints/Objects.json#TheRooted]

Warden Nossik
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Catacomb.json · CatacombWarden_1]

D603 · Opening / return
WARDEN NOSSIK:
You came in over the dew, so we knew. Speak low; the stone carries. I am Nossik, and I keep the way in. What is your purpose here?

  PLAYER 1: I'm only passing through.
    Continue at D604 — Passing.
  PLAYER 2: What is this place?
    Continue at D605 — Place.
  PLAYER 3: What are the rules here?
    Continue at D606 — Rules.
  PLAYER 4: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D604 · Passing
WARDEN NOSSIK:
Another way to see it is that nobody passes through a village; they pass through the people in it, and are remembered or not. Walk where the light is. Do not put your hands in the patch.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D603 — Start.

D605 · Place
WARDEN NOSSIK:
A village. Count us if you like — we are enough to keep the patch and not enough to be interesting. The wall is the older half of us. The Tender is at it now; she will talk longer than you want, and you should let her.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D603 — Start.

D606 · Rules
WARDEN NOSSIK:
Two. Speak low — a voice at surface-volume is a shout down here, and we will think there is a fire. And leave the patch alone. Not as a courtesy. The patch is the warmth, the food, the argument and the god's sleep in one body; a hand laid on it wrongly is not theft, it is war, and I would not be able to stop what came after.

  PLAYER 1: Understood.
    Continue at D603 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

The Plaque-Tender
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Catacomb.json · PlaqueTender_1]

D607 · Opening / return
THE PLAQUE-TENDER:
Mind the lamp; I have it low for the bottom rows, and the bottom rows have earned it. You may read. Most walkers read the top and think they have read a wall.

  PLAYER 1: Tell me about the wall.
    Continue at D608 — Wall.
  PLAYER 2: There's a smooth place, with nothing on it.
    Continue at D609 — Smooth.
  PLAYER 3: Who was Merrin?
    Continue at D611 — Merrin.
  PLAYER 4: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

D608 · Wall
THE PLAQUE-TENDER:
Oldest at the floor, newest at the ceiling, and every one of them re-cut before the letters go soft. That is the whole of my work and most of my life. You see the plaque at the top of row nine — Riane, the deep cut? She's my grandmother's grandmother. Hers says the patch sang. It did sing. We just don't say so in front of guests.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D607 — Start.

D609 · Smooth
THE PLAQUE-TENDER:
You'll ask about the smooth place. Everyone asks. That was a man who sold the tunnel-map of this village to walkers who came back with knives. The council voted refusal-to-inscribe. I won't say his name; that's the point of it; he isn't anywhere now, not even here. I'll tell you this much — my mother's mother voted against. She said no wall should have a hole in it. She's row eleven; you can tell her you agree, if you do.

  PLAYER 1: I think she was right.
    Continue at D610 — Agree.
  PLAYER 2: Back.
    Continue at D607 — Start.

D610 · Agree
THE PLAQUE-TENDER:
Then go and say it to row eleven, not to me. She is the one who has been waiting to hear it.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D607 — Start.

D611 · Merrin
THE PLAQUE-TENDER:
One winter, and called Moth for the way she went at the lamps. We re-cut her plaque every generation; the letters must never wear past reading. That one is the whole wall's business, not just her line's. The light dimmed the night she went. The old people say it was courtesy. I was not old then and I say so too.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D607 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

Listening elder
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/FoundingVillage.json · FoundingListener_1]

D612 · Opening / return
LISTENING ELDER:
Mind the lamp; your sleeve is close. This is the Rooted. That's his living plume you're looking at. We sit here when the light is low.

  PLAYER 1: What is he reaching for?
    Continue at D613 — Wall.
  PLAYER 2: How may I be trusted here?
    Continue at D614 — Trust.
  PLAYER 3: May I sleep on the plume?
    Available when: Liked or better with Catacomb Folk.
    Continue at D615 — Rest.
  PLAYER 4: I dreamed in his arms.
    Available when: fact RootedMet:=:1.
    Continue at D617 — Dream.
  PLAYER 5: You recognized me before I spoke.
    Available when: Founding Bloom.
    Continue at D616 — Bloom.
  PLAYER 6: Leave quietly.
    Conversation ends.

D613 · Wall
LISTENING ELDER:
The First Root is beyond the eastern wall. A long way through the stone. He's had his arms open toward it for a thousand years. We leave the space before his fingertips clear; there's room to walk around.

  PLAYER 1: I will.
    Continue at D612 — Start.

D614 · Trust
LISTENING ELDER:
The tender is working at our oldest wall. She needs a chip of tepuibone for a crumbling edge. There are pale seams upstairs, on the Stump's slopes. Take her one and stay while she speaks the name. If you've hurt a hearth, she won't forget it for a stone.

  PLAYER 1: I will speak with the tender.
    Continue at D612 — Start.

D615 · Rest
LISTENING ELDER:
There is room beside his chest. Lie on the living plume, near the light. Wait until it's quiet; nobody sleeps well with trouble at the door. I keep a lamp here for waking guests. You can sit a while before you tell anyone what you've heard.

  PLAYER 1: I will be gentle.
    Conversation ends.

D616 · Bloom
LISTENING ELDER:
Patch-bloom. My sister carried it for weeks after she slept here. I knew you'd been spoken to. I do not know what he said; that belongs to you.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D612 — Start.

D617 · Dream
LISTENING ELDER:
Come and sit on the warm side. You need not make the fragments into a speech for me. If you want to talk later, I'll be here.

  PLAYER 1: Thank you.
    Conversation ends.

Founding-wall tender
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/FoundingVillage.json · FoundingPlaqueTender_1]

D618 · Opening / return
FOUNDING-WALL TENDER:
Keep the lamp low, please. My mother taught me this row. The edge is giving way under the last syllable; a chip of tepuibone would brace it while I work. Bring one from the Stump's slopes, if you can. Stay while I speak the name. It's easier to keep one with someone listening.

  PLAYER 1: Place one tepuibone chip beside the inscription and listen.
    Available when: Can Offer Founding Stone.
    Continue at D618 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Tell me how you tend him.
    Continue at D619 — Tending.
  PLAYER 3: We kept the inscription together.
    Available when: fact FoundingStoneOffered:=:1.
    Continue at D620 — Kept.
  PLAYER 4: Leave the lamp where it is.
    Conversation ends.

D619 · Tending
FOUNDING-WALL TENDER:
I wipe the stalks and turn the lamps, as I would at any of our hearths. That light is coming from his chest. Mind your blade near it, please. Hurt him and you won't find a welcome at our other hearths either.

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D618 — Start.

D620 · Kept
FOUNDING-WALL TENDER:
The edge is holding. Thank you for staying. If you're tired, speak to the elder; he'll help you find a place beside the light.

  PLAYER 1: Leave quietly.
    Conversation ends.

========================================================================
11 · CHOOSING WHAT THE WORLD WILL KEEP
========================================================================
  [Ending preparation]
These offers prepare alternatives. Gathered takes a Choir cutting; Kept takes three name-holding stones. Either commitment can be ended aloud. Taking both is possible, but only one world ending can be enacted.


Choir tendril
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/RotChoir.json · ChoirTendril_1]

D621 · Thread
CHOIR TENDRIL:
'A thread. Yes. We have been walking there for a thousand years, a thread a season; you could carry one down in an afternoon.' Something pale and green separates from the tendril and rests in the air between you, warm. 'You should know what it costs before you close your hand, because the rememberers will say we never tell it. Gathered: every self untied, every thread kept. Nothing is forgotten. No one remains -- not you, not us as we are, not the six who struck. That is the whole of what it costs, and the whole of what it keeps. Of the seventh we sing all six accounts and take no side; the Root will sing them too. If you set this down, tell any of us. The soil is patient, and so are we.'

  PLAYER 1: I'll carry it.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: Not from me.
    Continue at D233 — Start.

D622 · Gather Released
CHOIR TENDRIL:
'Then it is set down, and said. We do not argue. The soil remembers that you said it to our face, which is more than most do.' The thread you did not carry has already grown back.

  PLAYER 1: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

Hollin Vesk
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/StillleafArchive.json · StillleafSearcher_1]

D623 · Stone
HOLLIN VESK:
Hollin sets the survey map aside. 'Kept. The Reader would have it so, and the Mainline would file the whole world under continuing. I will tell you the margin first, since no one else will: a kept world heals nothing. The estranged stay estranged; the tired stay tired; and the Six stay exactly as they are, forever. That is the whole of what it keeps.' She works a chip loose from the archive wall with the back of a knife and does not look at it. 'Memory-marble. It holds what is written harder than the hand. If you mean to seal the Root you will need tepuibone and mute-stone beside it; tepuibone lies loose where the Root is, and Curation keeps mute-stone. And if you set this down, tell me, not the stone.'

  PLAYER 1: I'll carry it.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: Not from me.
    Continue at D493 — Start.

D624 · Keep Released
HOLLIN VESK:
Hollin writes one line and turns the map back toward herself. 'Entered. The world stays unkept a while longer; it has practice at that. Thank you for saying so to my face.'

  PLAYER 1: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

Teodra Halm
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/StillleafArchive.json · StillleafIndexer_1]

D625 · Mute Stone
TEODRA HALM:
The gloved hand stops. 'The sealing. Filed under continuing - all of it, forever.' Teodra Halm looks up for the first time. 'You should know what that costs before you ask me for the stone. A kept world does not heal. The estranged stay estranged, the tired stay tired, and the file is never closed. Curation prefers it. I am telling you the margin anyway.' A drawer opens; a pale grey stone comes out wrapped in salt-cloth. 'Mute-stone. It does not answer, so nothing can unsay it. Tepuibone lies loose where the Root is; memory-marble is the archivists' to give. If you change your mind, say so here. The file does not editorialise, but it records.'

  PLAYER 1: I'll carry it.
    Conversation ends.
  PLAYER 2: Not from me.
    Continue at D497 — Start.

D626 · Keep Released
TEODRA HALM:
'Entered: released, at the walker's word.' The gloved hand does not pause. 'The file records a no as exactly as a yes. Curation does not keep margins.'

  PLAYER 1: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

========================================================================
12 · AT THE SEVENTH INTERVAL AND THE ROOT
========================================================================
  [Final choices]
Two Renewal choices are enacted at the Felling-Site’s empty seventh position. Gathered and Kept are enacted beside the taproot’s face beneath the Root, two map cells north. All four share one irreversible ending state; the following chapters are alternatives.


The empty seventh position
  [Examine before an ending is enacted.]
READING / EXAMINATION:
A seventh interval in the circle. Nothing occupies it. Looking across it, the near and far edges briefly cease to agree. The air is wrong at this one point.

  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Blueprints/Objects.json#SeventhPosition]

The sleeping Root
  [Examine the Root before an ending is enacted.]
READING / EXAMINATION:
The taproot's face, exposed where the stone gave way: sandstone-pale, warm to a hand laid flat, slower than breathing. It is the last living root of the Tree that held everything, and it is asleep. It dreams. What it dreams, no one who came down here has been able to say.

  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Blueprints/Objects.json#TheRoot]

Choice: the Strike
  [Before any ending; at the seventh position.]
PLAYER CHOICE:
be struck as seventh: it cracks one day

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/EndingSpine.cs:45]

Choice: naming by practice
  [Before any ending; at the seventh position.]
PLAYER CHOICE:
name the world: clean ledger, gods end

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/EndingSpine.cs:46]

An open ledger
  [Attempt practice-path Renewal with open acts. Runtime appends descriptions of each open act.]
NARRATION:
You cannot teach the world to finish its names while leaving your own unspoken: 

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/EndingSpine.cs:58]

  [Before any ending; beside the Root face.]

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/EndingRoutes.cs:75]

R346 · Narration
  [TryWorldAction / post-ending examination]

The face is under three stones now - tepuibone, memory-marble, mute-stone - set flush against it and already cold. It does not dream. It is kept, and the keeping must never stop.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/EndingRoutes.cs:76]

========================================================================
13 · RENEWED: THE VESSEL
========================================================================
  [Alternative ending]
ALTERNATIVE ENDING — choose only this branch or one of the other three. The bearer accepts the Strike at the seventh interval. Naming holds as long as the bearer keeps choosing; the epilogue acknowledges the far-generation crack.

  [This is an alternative ending. It does not follow the previous ending in the same playthrough.]

Vessel-path Renewal
  [Ending:=:1; accept the Strike at the seventh position.]
ENDING NARRATION:
You stand in the seventh interval and let the Strike come. It does not install a god; it installs a chooser. The world re-binds around a bearer; a new Tree grows; the sari... sari... stops, and green returns to the tepui's edges. It holds for as long as you keep choosing it, every morning, for as long as you have mornings — and no one can choose it for you after. Somewhere far down the generations the yes will wear into habit, and habit cannot hold Naming. That is the crack. It is not today.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/EndingSpine.cs:27]

After this ending: ChoirTendril_1
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/RotChoir.json · ChoirTendril_1]
Optional return-visit response; only Ending:=:1.

'A bearer. We felt the new binding close over the soil like skin over a cut. We are still here, walker, and still hungry, and still patient. So is a bearer, for a while.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D233 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.

D629 · After1

Hollin does not look up. 'A bearer. The survey maps agree with each other this morning, which they have not done in my lifetime. I have entered it. I have also entered, in the margin, that nobody asked the bearer what a thousand mornings cost. Someone should read that, later.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.

========================================================================
14 · RENEWED: THE PRACTICE
========================================================================
  [Alternative ending]
ALTERNATIVE ENDING — choose only this branch or one of the other three. With every undertaken act carried through or refused aloud, the player refuses the Strike and names the world. The Six begin to age; the work continues; the Root dissolves into the binding.

  [This is an alternative ending. It does not follow the previous ending in the same playthrough.]

Practice-path Renewal
  [Ending:=:2; name the world at the seventh position with a clean closure-ledger.]
ENDING NARRATION:
You stand in the seventh interval and refuse it aloud, the way the first no was refused, and you say the world's name back to it instead. No vessel. The empty seventh is answered by a practice: every guest named under the cloth, every friend named by a child, every grandmother's meadow, all of it doing the work, all the time, everywhere. The pressure drains at its source. The costs are permanent and you will witness them: the Six begin to age; the work never finishes; the Root, told it will never hold alone again, wakes without fear and dissolves into the new binding. The flowers last longer here. So do the funerals.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/EndingSpine.cs:32]

After this ending: ChoirTendril_1
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/RotChoir.json · ChoirTendril_1]
Optional return-visit response; only Ending:=:2.


D631 · After2
CHOIR TENDRIL:
'Everyone names everyone now. It tickles, down in the substrate. We are learning to be named back. We do not know yet whether we like it; we are finding out slowly, which is how we find out everything.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D233 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

After this ending: PalimpsestEcho_1
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/Palimpsest.json · PalimpsestEcho_1]
Optional return-visit response; only Ending:=:2.


D632 · After2
RECENSION FIELD SCRIBE:
'Every margin in the archive is filling with names, and none of them are ours. I have never been so glad to be crowded out. The third hand's entries were quiet about this. They still are.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D274 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

After this ending: StillleafSearcher_1
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/StillleafArchive.json · StillleafSearcher_1]

'Everyone is naming everything. The Recension has never been busier or less needed.' She turns a page. 'They say the Reader is tired. The Reader has never been tired. I have entered it as rumour, which is all the Mainline will let me call it.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D493 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.

========================================================================
15 · GATHERED
========================================================================
  [Alternative ending]
ALTERNATIVE ENDING — choose only this branch or one of the other three. The carried Choir cutting reaches the Root. Every thread is kept and every individual self is lost.

  [This is an alternative ending. It does not follow the previous ending in the same playthrough.]

Gathered
  [Ending:=:3; set a carried ChoirCutting against the Root’s face.]
ENDING NARRATION:
You set the cutting against the face and it takes, the way water takes the way to the sea. The Choir reaches the Root through the substrate, and the Wedded ascends to sole divinity; the pantheon collapses to one. The world becomes a single warm devouring memory - there is room in us for everyone - every self untied and its threads re-woven into the great weave. Nothing is forgotten; no one remains. The bio-light goes out across every catacomb-village as the Rooted fades, then returns, changed, as the glow of the new Tree-Choir. The sari... sari... stops: a world with one name has no seams left for the pressure to work. The flower-charms bloom, briefly, in the few unconsumed pockets, and then those bloom into the Choir too. The kindest apocalypse and the most total: a world that remembers everything and is no one, and the only one, of the possible worlds, in which nothing is ever lost again.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/EndingRoutes.cs:35]

After this ending: ChoirTendril_1
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/RotChoir.json · ChoirTendril_1]
Optional return-visit response; only Ending:=:3.


D635 · After3
CHOIR TENDRIL:
'You carried us down. We are grateful, and soon there will be no one to be grateful to: only us, and us is everyone. Do not be afraid of the green. It remembers you already.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D233 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.

========================================================================
16 · KEPT
========================================================================
  [Alternative ending]
ALTERNATIVE ENDING — choose only this branch or one of the other three. The three stones seal the Root. The Thinning halts, nothing heals, and the Six remain themselves.

  [This is an alternative ending. It does not follow the previous ending in the same playthrough.]

Kept
  [Ending:=:4; seal the Root carrying Tepuibone, MemoryMarble and MuteStone.]
ENDING NARRATION:
You set tepuibone, memory-marble and mute-stone against the face, the three that hold their names hardest, and the Root is sealed. The Thinning halts. The seal must be tended forever, and it will be; you have made a vigil, not a cure, because you cannot heal a tiring thing by stopping its clock. The world is held still: nothing decays, and nothing heals. The estranged stay estranged. The tired stay tired, unable to die. The festival-flowers last the afternoon and not a minute more, and never will again. And the Six survive as themselves - the Reader still reading, the Rooted still reaching, every god you have come to know still there, exactly as they are, forever. That is not nothing. It is everything, kept.

  [Source: Assets/Scripts/Gameplay/World/EndingRoutes.cs:29]

After this ending: ChoirTendril_1
  [Source: Assets/Resources/Content/Conversations/RotChoir.json · ChoirTendril_1]
Optional return-visit response; only Ending:=:4.


D639 · After4
CHOIR TENDRIL:
'Sealed. We cannot reach it now. We will wait. The soil is patient, and so are we, longer than a seal, longer than the ones who tend it.'

  PLAYER 1: Back.
    Continue at D233 — Start.
  PLAYER 2: Farewell.
    Conversation ends.
